Renamed `stunnedRounds` → `stunnedValue` everywhere in personnel-engine (source, tests, the Erabryth fixture, `stunDenial`'s own per-unit price, rescaled from 3.0/round to 1.0/action to keep the same rough real-world magnitude) — **deliberately NOT touched at Frame scale**, where `stunnedRounds` is a different, older, pre-existing pattern used only by hardcoded narrative scripted-events (`target.stunnedRounds = 2` as an authored story beat, e.g. a Claimant's Sweep), never derived from a real creature's own "Stunned N" text — conflating the two would have been a scope creep this report never asked for. The artifact viewer's own turn-overview and narration panels previously assumed `stunned` always meant "zero actions, nothing to show" — fixed to render the real actions a partially-stunned turn still took, with a "lost N action(s) to Stunned" note instead of the old blanket "loses this turn."

### Bug 2 — movement was blocked by allies, not just enemies

Ingram's own correction, with a citation: *"Characters can move through friendly character spaces per the rules."* Checked directly: `isOccupied` (and everything built on it — `legality.mjs`'s path validation, `world/pathfind.mjs`'s Dijkstra flood, `decide.mjs`'s straight-line fallback) treated ANY occupied square as a hard block, ally or enemy alike, with no side check anywhere. This wasn't just a legality gap; it plausibly starved the AI's own candidate generation of real routes whenever a teammate happened to be standing between it and anywhere useful — a real, if partial, contributor to the exact "wastes a turn shuffling" pattern under review.

Fixed with a new `blocksPassage(pos, occupiedPositions, moverSide)` (`grid.mjs`), matching `isOccupied`'s shape but only counting a DIFFERENT-side occupant as blocking — a same-side occupant is a real waypoint, not a wall, matching the real rule's other half too (*"though you can't stop there"* — the actual DESTINATION square is still checked with a plain, side-blind `isOccupied`, so nobody can ever end a move standing on an ally either). Threaded through every layer that previously called `isOccupied` for a movement check: `world/pathfind.mjs`'s `reachableCells` (explores THROUGH a same-side cell so everything beyond it is still reachable, but never offers that cell itself as a selectable destination — the same explore-vs-select split the earlier corpse-in-doorway fix, §22, already established for HP-dead combatants), `decide.mjs`'s `extend`/`stepToward`/`stepAway` (the no-walls straight-line fallback), and `legality.mjs`'s `checkMoveLegality` (both the supplied-path and straight-line-fallback validation branches). `moverSide == null` (no side info available at all) safely falls back to the original any-occupant-blocks rule rather than silently treating every occupant as passable. Verified at every layer: unit tests on `blocksPassage` itself, `reachableCells` proving a route through an ally costs the true straight-line distance rather than detouring around, and `bestDestinationToward` proving the real entry point the AI calls walks straight past an ally but still genuinely detours around an enemy in the identical spot.

### Bug 3 — a cover object could seal a combatant inside it, and one actually did

*"Nitpick's first turn is three 5ft moves in a row... They start on top of a table... Are they stuck? Moving across cover should be possible, spawning inside shouldn't happen."* Investigated directly rather than assumed: `coverBlock`'s own four-edge perimeter used `move:20` (fully solid, identical to a real wall) on all four sides with no doorway — a small object is therefore a SEALED BOX, walkable INTO but never back OUT of. Cross-checked against the actual map data: the `SAME_ROOM` setup's own Bennit spawn point, `{col:15,row:20}`, sat exactly inside `coverBlock(14,19,2,2)`'s 2x2 interior. She really was trapped from turn 1, and the "three 5ft moves" were her own pathfinding correctly finding nothing better among the only 2-4 cells she could legally reach inside her own cage.

Fixed at the actual root, not just at that one coordinate: `coverBlock` no longer blocks movement at all (`move:0`). This engine has no difficult-terrain cost model to charge for climbing real waist-high cover (a crate, a low barrier), so full permeability is the honest simplification Ingram's own framing asked for — *"moving across cover should be possible"* — not `move:20` with a hand-placed gap that would only reinvent the same trap under different coordinates the next time a map got redesigned. `hasCover`'s own AC/Reflex bonus is completely unaffected (it was always independent of `move`, per its own header) — cover still makes you harder to hit, it just no longer makes you unable to walk anywhere. A dedicated regression test asserts every cover object on the real map is fully walkable, not just the one that trapped Bennit specifically.

### Structural redesign — opposite corners, a real exterior, more doors/windows/garage openings

*"Spawn the two sides at opposite corners of the map, together so that they have access to a full band of space outside the buildings at least two grid spaces wide. By putting them in opposite corners we force them to find each other first. ... Add more external doors to both buildings, add more windows. Add some large garage like openings."*

Checked first, and confirmed real: the map had **no exterior space at all** — both buildings spanned the grid's full height with zero north/south buffer, and were flush against the west/east edges too. There was no "outside" for an opposite-corners spawn to exist in. Fixed by growing the grid from 38x24 to **44x30** and shifting both buildings +3 cols/+3 rows, producing a uniform >=3-square exterior perimeter band on every side — comfortably over the ">= 2 grid spaces" floor, verified by test rather than eyeballed off the new dimensions.

`SETUPS` was rebuilt from scratch around this new exterior band, not adapted from the old room-based ones — the whole premise changed from "start already inside a room, sometimes adjacent to a shared door" to "start at an opposite corner or end of the whole map, with real ground to cover before any shot is possible." Three real setups, genuinely different shapes, all verified by connectivity/line-of-effect test rather than assumed:
- **`DIAGONAL_SW_NE`** and **`DIAGONAL_NW_SE`** — true opposite corners; a real building plausibly sits in the direct path, so neither side starts with line of effect to the other.
- **`NORTH_SOUTH_ENDS`** — opposite ends along the open courtyard gap between the two buildings; the deliberate exception, where both sides genuinely *can* see each other at spawn (the courtyard has nothing in it to block that), still 135ft apart — real, intentional tactical variety (an open-field approach), not an oversight, and pinned as its own explicit test rather than silently contradicting the other two setups' own "no initial line of effect" invariant.

The old setup names (`SAME_ROOM`/`SAME_BUILDING_FAR_ROOMS`/`ACROSS_THE_COMPLEX`) no longer described anything real once spawning moved outside the buildings entirely, so they were renamed rather than kept as a stale, misleading label — cascading into `run-personnel-scenario.mjs`'s scenario keys (`complex-3v3-diagonal-sw-ne`/`-diagonal-nw-se`/`-north-south-ends`, superseding §23's `complex-3v3-same-room` etc.) and every test that referenced them by name. `roundLimit` raised from 30 to 40 across the board — opposite-corner spawns require substantially more real travel before combat can even start.

New doors, windows, and garage openings (design doc §24's own literal ask), added to both buildings rather than just one: Building A gained a second external door (south wall, into the SE room) and a second window (NE room, north wall); Building B gained a second external door (north wall) and a second window (east wall). Both buildings gained a real "garage" opening — mechanically just an extra-wide (3-square) doorway, blocking neither movement nor sight, on Building A's west wall (SW room) and Building B's east wall (south room) respectively — visually and tactically distinct from a standard 1-square door without needing any new wall-schema mechanism.

Every new opening, every renamed setup, and the "no spawn point ever overlaps a cover object again" invariant are covered by real, passing tests in `test/battle-simulator/personnel-test-complex.test.mjs` (fully rewritten) — not just the map file itself.

**One more real finding, from actually running the redesigned map, not just checking its geometry in isolation.** A test proving "real cover gets used in real 3v3 fights" (added when cover was first built, §22) started failing once the new setups landed — not a broken assertion, a real, measured fact: across a fresh sweep of real battles on every one of the three new setups, not one single attack's line ever crossed a cover object. Every cover object on this map predates the opposite-corners redesign and sits deep inside a specific room (the NW hall, the SW room, each building's own courtyard-door corner) — real, useful placements for the OLD room-based spawns, which started combatants already adjacent to them. Once spawning moved to opposite map corners, real fights increasingly resolve in the open courtyard the two buildings flank, or wherever the two sides first make contact along the way — nowhere near where the old cover happened to be built. Cover didn't stop working; it just stopped mattering, because nothing was left where combat actually happens now. Fixed by adding two real cover objects directly in the courtyard itself (`cols 21-29`, the one stretch of ground every setup's own travel genuinely passes through or near, corner or end, diagonal or not) rather than patching the test to expect less. The specific test that caught this dropped from a ~73-second, all-10-seeds, never-finds-cover run to under 10 seconds once real courtyard cover existed to find.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **A genuinely different Stunned-with-a-DURATION variant** (Player Core's own alternate form — "stunned for 1 minute," lose every action for that span) — no real fixture in this project uses it, so it wasn't built speculatively alongside the value-based fix Erabryth's real "Stunned N" text actually needed.
- **Stunned/Slowed interaction** (the real rule's own worked example covers what happens when both apply at once) — no fixture currently carries Slowed at all, so there's nothing yet for this interaction to apply to; noted as a real gap for whenever one does, not invented now.
- **Tumble Through** (moving through a HOSTILE creature's space via an Acrobatics check, Ingram's own citation) — only the ally-passthrough half of the real movement rule was built. A real, separate action this engine doesn't model yet; moving through an ENEMY's space still requires going around, same as before.

### Bug 4 (small) — the map and tokens rendered squashed

A one-line root cause, found by reading the CSS directly rather than guessing: `.map-wrap`'s `aspect-ratio` was hardcoded to `1 / 1`, a leftover from when every map (the 44x44 Frame-scale city district) happened to be square. The new personnel-scale complex map is 44x30 — genuinely NOT square — so the container silently stretched the SVG (already rendering with `preserveAspectRatio="none"`, correct once the container itself is the right shape) non-uniformly to fit, squashing the whole map and turning every circular token into an oval along whichever axis got compressed. Fixed by setting `#mapWrap`'s `aspect-ratio` dynamically in `selectRun` (`${COLS} / ${ROWS}`, geometry.mjs's own already-correct per-run scale) rather than a static CSS value — the fix belongs where the real shape is actually known (per-run, at selection time), not in a stylesheet that has no way to know it in advance.

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/engine.mjs` (the real Stunned mechanic — `runTurn`'s `actionsAvailableOverride`, the round loop's partial-vs-full-forfeit split, `stunnedValue` rename throughout), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/evaluate/state-value.mjs` (`stunDenial` rescaled to per-action), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/grid.mjs` (`blocksPassage`), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/world/pathfind.mjs` (`reachableCells`'s `moverSide`, explore-vs-select split), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/decide.mjs` (`otherLivingPositions` now carries `side`; `extend`/`stepToward`/`stepAway`/`pathfindingDestinationToward`/`pathfindingDestinationAway` all faction-aware), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/legality.mjs` (`checkMoveLegality`'s intermediate-square checks), `tools/battle-simulator/data/personnel-test-complex.mjs` (fully rewritten — 44x30 grid, real exterior perimeter, `coverBlock` no longer move-blocking, new doors/windows/garage openings, `SETUPS` rebuilt around opposite corners/ends), `tools/battle-simulator/run-personnel-scenario.mjs` (renamed `complex-3v3-*` scenario keys), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (`#mapWrap`'s dynamic aspect-ratio; stunned-turn rendering shows real partial actions), `tools/character-builder/generate-raw-validation-fixtures.mjs` (`stunnedValue` rename in Erabryth's outcomes table), plus test coverage for every one of the above (`personnel-engine.test.mjs`, `personnel-grid.test.mjs`, `personnel-pathfind.test.mjs`, `personnel-decide.test.mjs`, `personnel-legality.test.mjs`, `personnel-state-value.test.mjs`, `personnel-test-complex.test.mjs`, `terrain-extensions.test.mjs`, `run-personnel-scenario-cli.test.mjs`, `personnel-complex-battle.test.mjs`, `raw-validation-fixtures.test.mjs`, `extract-npc.test.mjs` all touched).

## 25. Conditions, PC dying/wounded/unconscious, and off-guard/flanking

Ingram's direct instruction, immediately after §24 shipped: *"research the rules then implement conditions, death/dying for PCs and off guard / flanking."* Real Player Core text first, mirrored into `reference/srd/player-core/` alongside the existing dying/healing article before any code changed — `423-flanking.json`, `condition-28-off-guard.json`, `condition-32-prone.json`, `condition-11-dying.json`, `condition-45-wounded.json`, `condition-40-unconscious.json` — same honest RETRIEVAL/MIRROR-NOTE disclosure this mirror has used throughout for freshly-fetched (as opposed to already-verbatim) text.

### Architecture: flat scalars and a handful of shared functions, not a registry

The temptation for "implement conditions" as a generic feature is a data-driven conditions registry. Rejected deliberately: the engine's own AI lookahead sandbox (`cloneStateForPlanning`) clones combatant state with a shallow `{...c}` spread, which already copies a flat scalar field correctly by value with zero special handling — a condition nested under its own `conditions: {}` object would alias between live battle state and a sandboxed planning fork, letting mere *planning* a hypothetical action mutate the real fight. Frightened and Stunned already proved the flat-scalar shape works; §24's own Stunned bug was never about the shape, it was a rule implemented as a bespoke inline snippet with nobody checking it against the real text. So every new condition — `prone`, `dyingValue`, `woundedValue` — is one more flat field, same precedent, and what's actually shared across conditions (the redundant-conditions stacking rule, "am I off-guard right now," "what's my own attack penalty right now," the knockout/worsen/recovery/revival transitions) lives once in a new `personnel-engine/conditions.mjs`, so a future condition extends a short list of pure functions instead of hand-copying `-(combatant.frightened ?? 0)` a third time. `isUnconscious`/`isDead` are computed, never stored — both are fully determined by `currentHP` + `side` + `dyingValue`, and a redundant stored flag is exactly the kind of thing that goes out of sync with the truth, which is the precise shape of bug the Stunned fix already found once.

### Off-Guard, Prone, and the redundant-conditions rule

Off-Guard (`condition-28-off-guard.json`) is a flat -2 circumstance penalty to AC, granted persistently by Prone and by Unconscious and — separately, geometrically, per-attack — by flanking. Prone (`condition-32-prone.json`) additionally applies its own -2 circumstance penalty to the prone creature's own attack rolls, stacking additively with Frightened's status penalty since they're different penalty sources, not the same named condition reapplied. Player Core pg. 435's "Redundant Conditions With Values" rule — reapplying a valued condition takes the higher value, never adds — is centralized as `applyValuedCondition(combatant, field, value)` and used everywhere a condition might stack against itself, EXCEPT Wounded, whose own text is a real, explicit exception: it increases by 1 each time rather than taking the max, modeled as its own `resolveRecoveryCheck` increment rather than routed through the shared max-wins helper.

One real, non-obvious modeling gap a test caught before it shipped: Unconscious's own text is *"you take a -4 status penalty to AC, Perception, and Reflex saves, AND you have the blinded and off-guard conditions"* — two distinct effects, not one. A status penalty and a circumstance penalty are different bonus types in SF2E and DO stack; an unconscious combatant's real AC penalty is -6 total (-4 status, -2 circumstance from off-guard), not -2. `combatant.mjs`'s `resolveEffectiveStats` originally only folded in the -2; a real-battle integration test asserting the exact expected AC (`target.ac - 2 - 4`) failed until `unconsciousStatusPenalty` was added as its own term alongside the off-guard penalty.

### The dying/wounded/unconscious spiral — PCs only, matching real RAW

Player Core pg. 402-404 (already fully mirrored at `reference/srd/player-core/367-hit-points-healing-and-dying.json` from earlier work): *"Player characters, their companions, and other significant characters... don't automatically die when they reach 0 Hit Points... The GM might determine that villains, powerful monsters, special NPCs... can use these rules as well."* Ordinary opposition in this engine dies outright at 0 HP — this isn't a simplification invented for this pass, it's the real rule's own asymmetry, and it's exactly what Ingram's own "for PCs" scoping already matched without having to be told the RAW justification.

`applyKnockout` (a `side: "party"` combatant hitting 0 HP for the first time): dying 1, or dying 2 on a critical hit, PLUS the current Wounded value if any — Player Core's own worked example (dying 2 while already Wounded 2 = dying 4 = instant death) is a literal passing test. The knocked-out combatant falls prone. `worsenDyingFromDamage` handles the OTHER real case — taking a fresh hit while already dying increases the dying value by 1 (2 on a crit) INSTEAD of further HP loss, since HP can't go below 0. `resolveRecoveryCheck` is the real flat check (d20, no modifiers, DC 10 + current dying value) rolled at the start of each of a dying PC's own turns: critical success -2, success -1, failure +1, critical failure +2, floored at 0; reaching 0 gains or increases Wounded by 1 (a real increment, not a reapplication — deliberately NOT routed through the max-wins helper); reaching 4 is death. It reuses the already-tested `degreeOfSuccess` from `engine/resolve.mjs` rather than hand-rolling nat-20/nat-1 boundaries a second time, since a flat check is just an ordinary check whose total happens to equal the raw die. `applyRevival` (restored to 1+ HP by any means) clears dying and prone but deliberately leaves Wounded untouched — Player Core is explicit that Wounded only ends via Treat Wounds or a 10-minute rest, neither of which happens by being healed back up mid-fight.

The round loop's biggest structural change: a `side: "party"` combatant at 0 HP but not yet dead gets a real recovery-check turn at the start of each of their own turns, BEFORE the existing Stunned machinery even runs (there's nothing to decide, so it isn't offered to the AI as a choice) — not skipped, not silently absent from the log, the same "expose the real mechanic instead of an empty row" discipline §24 already established for partial Stunned turns.

**Three proactive fixes, found by reading the surrounding code before any test caught them.** Building the whole dying/revival mechanic would have been pointless without checking whether the rest of the engine could actually act on it: `checkTargetAlive` and `checkHealAbilityLegal` (`legality.mjs`) both pre-dated this feature and blanket-rejected any target at `currentHP <= 0` — meaning a downed PC could be neither finished off nor healed, legally, even after all of the above shipped. `decide.mjs`'s `generateProposals` filtered its own `living` candidate list the same way, which would have silently kept the AI from ever proposing either action against a downed PC even once the legality layer allowed it. All three switched from the `currentHP <= 0` shorthand to the real `isDead()` check.

### Flanking

Player Core pg. 417 (`423-flanking.json`): attacker and ally on opposite sides or opposite corners of a target grant it off-guard against melee attacks from the two flankers specifically — both must be able to act, both need a melee option in reach of the target. `isFlanking` (`personnel-engine/flanking.mjs`) checks this as same-line-opposite-direction via `Math.sign()` on both grid axes, generalized mid-build from an initial exact-midpoint version that a failing test caught: exact midpoint mathematically requires both flankers to stand equidistant from the target, which breaks the instant either one carries a reach weapon and stands farther back. `engine.mjs`'s strike branch applies the resulting -2 only to melee strikes, and only when the target isn't already off-guard from Prone/Unconscious (composing the two off-guard sources by boolean OR, never by stacking a second -2, confirmed by a real-battle test asserting the exact AC total). Not modeled, and flagged rather than silently skipped: deny advantage (a rogue class feature no current fixture has) and all-around-vision immunity — real, documented gaps matching this project's own don't-build-ahead-of-need convention, not oversights.

### Debugging the tests, not the engine — four real test-authoring bugs, none of them in the mechanic

The first full run of the new integration test file (`personnel-dying.test.mjs`) showed 4 of 6 tests failing. Direct log inspection — not another round of guessing — found the actual causes, and every one of them was in the TEST setup, not `conditions.mjs`/`engine.mjs` themselves:

- Two tests built a battle with only ONE party combatant, who then got knocked down. `checkOutcome`'s own pre-existing rule (`party.every(c => c.currentHP <= 0)` -> party-defeat) fires the instant that lone PC drops, ending the battle before their own next turn — the recovery check they were meant to exercise — could ever happen. Fixed by adding a second, untouched party member to every dying-related test, matching how this actually plays out in the real 3v3 rosters this project cares about (one PC going down never alone ends a fight where two allies are still standing).
- One test built a battle with ZERO opposition combatants. `opposition.every(c => c.currentHP <= 0)` is vacuously true on an empty array — the battle ended in round 0, before anyone acted, misread at first as the healer's action never firing. Fixed by adding a harmless full-HP opposition bystander.
- One test's scripted AI hardcoded the attacker's id as `"attacker"` while the fixture actually named it `"pc-attacker"` — the custom AI silently never matched, the engine's own default decision-making took over instead, and the resulting turn order had nothing to do with what the test meant to check. Fixed by parameterizing the attacker id instead of hardcoding it.
- One assertion expected an opposition NPC to receive exactly zero turns once dead; it received one. Correct behavior, wrong assertion — if initiative puts the NPC's own turn before the killing blow lands later the same round, that earlier turn is real and legitimate. Fixed to assert "no turns AFTER death," not "no turns at all."

Worth recording plainly, in the same spirit as §22's "not one silently glossed over": none of these four were engine bugs. They're recorded here because a wrong test can look exactly like a real regression from the outside, and getting to the real root cause required reading actual log output each time rather than patching the assertion that happened to be red.

### The artifact viewer, and a real pre-existing bug found while extending it

Design doc §14f's own standing ask — "show a character sheet... so I can see where the numbers are coming from" — applies just as much to a downed PC as an acting one, so the roster panel, character-sheet header, round-overview turn boxes, screen narration, and the round header's status badge all gained real rendering for Dying N / Wounded N / Unconscious / Prone / Dead, distinguished from Frame-scale's own damage-state chip by whether `dyingValue` is present on the snapshot at all (personnel-scale always includes it, even at 0; Frame-scale never has the field). A downed-but-not-dead PC now shows a red "Unconscious — Dying N" chip and a warm-tinted card rather than the old full-opacity "dead" treatment — visually distinct from an actually-dead combatant, matching the real rule's own distinction between the two states.

Extending this surfaced a real, pre-existing bug independent of this feature: `artifact/steps.mjs`'s `buildSteps` flattens each round's raw turn objects into the linear step sequence the viewer scrubs through, through an explicit field whitelist — and `stunnedActionsLost` was never in it. §24's Stunned rework logged the real lost-action count on every turn, but the viewer could only ever show the boolean `stunned` flag; the actual NUMBER silently fell back to its own hardcoded default (`?? 3` / `?? 0`) every single time, since the flattening step dropped it before rendering ever saw it. Fixed alongside adding `dying`/`recoveryCheck` to the same whitelist (needed for this feature's own recovery-check turns to render as anything but an empty row) — same category of gap, fixed together, with new tests on `buildSteps` itself covering both the pre-existing gap and the new fields.

### Verification

`test/battle-simulator/personnel-conditions.test.mjs` (35 tests, pure-function layer), `personnel-flanking.test.mjs` (15 tests — 11 geometry unit tests plus 4 real-battle integration tests), `personnel-dying.test.mjs` (6 real-battle integration tests) and `test/battle-simulator/steps.test.mjs`'s new cases all pass, and the full `test/battle-simulator/` suite (674 tests before this pass) passes with zero regressions. Beyond unit coverage: eight fresh seeds of the real 3v3 `complex-3v3-diagonal-sw-ne` scenario were run end-to-end, and direct log inspection (not assumption) confirmed real dying/unconscious activity in the battles where it should occur (three of the eight ended in `party-defeat`, and every downed PC in those logs carried a real, non-degenerate `dyingValue` between 1 and 4, never stuck at a stale default). One seed's full transcript was read through the rebuilt artifact viewer in a real browser: Lunar-17 knocked to 0 HP in round 3, a real recovery check the following turn (`d20 17 vs DC 12, success, dying 2 -> 1`), narrated correctly across every panel — roster chip, character sheet, round-overview box, screen text, and the round header's "RECOVERY CHECK" badge — before eventually going down again alongside the rest of the party. Flanking never fired even once across all eight fresh seeds nor across the other 36 pre-existing logs in the store — plausible rather than alarming, since nothing in `decide.mjs`/`evaluate/state-value.mjs` yet gives the AI any reason to seek an opposite-side position deliberately, so it only happens by incidental positioning; the geometry itself is separately confirmed by its own 11 direct unit tests.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **AI flanking-awareness** — `evaluate/state-value.mjs` gives the AI no reason to seek a flanking position deliberately, which is almost certainly why it was never observed in real battles. A real, worthwhile follow-on, not attempted here to keep this pass scoped to the rules mechanic itself rather than a retuning pass.
- **A "dying denial" evaluator term** — prioritizing healing a badly-dying ally over other targets isn't modeled in the AI's own decision-making; healing still happens only when the existing evaluator logic already favors it.
- **A separate post-heal Stand action** — RAW, a revived character still needs to spend an action to stand up from Prone; `applyRevival` clears Prone directly instead, a deliberate, documented simplification since nothing in the current roster inflicts Prone through any path except this exact knockout/heal cycle, and modeling a standalone Stand action for a case with no other real source would be speculative machinery ahead of actual need.
- **Deny Advantage and all-around vision** (flanking immunities) — no current fixture has either, so neither was built speculatively alongside the geometry a real fixture does need.

### Files

New: `reference/srd/player-core/423-flanking.json`, `condition-28-off-guard.json`, `condition-32-prone.json`, `condition-11-dying.json`, `condition-45-wounded.json`, `condition-40-unconscious.json`; `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/conditions.mjs`; `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/flanking.mjs`; `test/battle-simulator/personnel-conditions.test.mjs`; `test/battle-simulator/personnel-flanking.test.mjs`; `test/battle-simulator/personnel-dying.test.mjs`. Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/combatant.mjs` (`resolveEffectiveStats` folds in off-guard and Unconscious's separate status penalty), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/legality.mjs` (`checkTargetAlive`/`checkHealAbilityLegal` use `isDead`), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/decide.mjs` (`generateProposals`'s `living` filter uses `isDead`), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/engine.mjs` (the recovery-check branch in the round loop, `applyDyingTransition` wired into strike/spellAttack/saveAttack, flanking's `flankBonus` in the strike branch, `snapshotState` extended), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/steps.mjs` (`stunnedActionsLost`/`dying`/`recoveryCheck` added to the flatten whitelist), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (condition chips in the roster/sheet panels, a recovery-check row in the round overview, recovery-check narration in the screen log and round header), `test/battle-simulator/steps.test.mjs` (new cases for the whitelist fix).

## 26. Real waypoint-accurate movement, and cover-seeking for ranged AI

Two independent reports, immediately after §25 shipped:

> *"we need to make moves valid from grid space to grid space, currently tokens push straight through walls which looks wrong. Grid space by grid space movement, special attention to moving accurately through doorways and around building corners."*

> *"once in contact with the enemy it would be smart for all AIs to consider the value of placing themselves behind cover if they are ranged. This needs to be done on the basis that this new being behind cover move would still allow them to be involved in the fight, no point hiding behind cover if your enemy outranges you or is currently not visible to you."*

### Bug — a mechanically-legal move was drawn as a straight line through whatever it had actually routed around

Checked directly before touching anything: `checkMoveLegality` (`personnel-engine/legality.mjs`) already validates a proposed move's real, wall-aware Dijkstra path cell-by-cell when one is supplied (`decide.mjs`'s `pathfindingDestinationToward`/`Away`, ported under design doc §16) — the MECHANIC was never wrong, a move genuinely could not cut through a wall or corner. The bug was entirely downstream: `engine.mjs`'s `attemptAction` destructured the real path OUT of the proposal (`const { path, ...to } = proposal.to;`) to keep it out of `state.positions`, but then never put it back INTO the logged action either — so the log carried only `from`/`to`, and the artifact viewer's `combatantWaypoints` had no choice but to draw a straight line between them. Frame-scale never surfaced this (no walled Frame-scale scenario exists yet, so its own identical `path`-discarding line was silently dead code), but personnel-scale's real complex map — buildings, doorways, corners, per §24's redesign — routes around real obstacles on nearly every Stride, so nearly every drawn line was wrong.

### Fix — preserve the real route, consume it at every layer that draws or animates one

`engine.mjs` (both scales — the Frame-scale copy has the identical latent bug, fixed alongside even though no walled scenario exercises it yet) now logs `path: path ?? null` on a legal move action. The artifact-viewer logic that turns that into pixels and animation — `actionLegs`/`combatantWaypoints` — was extracted out of template.html's inline script into a new `artifact/movement.mjs`, matching the exact precedent `geometry.mjs`/`steps.mjs`/`library.mjs` already set (design doc §15m): real, non-trivial logic belongs in a module `node:test` can cover directly, not only ever exercised by clicking through the UI. `combatantWaypoints` now walks every point in a move action's own logged path (falling back to `[from,to]` when none was logged — a wall-less scenario's straight-line fallback, where that already IS the real route) and pairs it with a per-leg TIME WEIGHT so a real 4-segment detour still animates in the same total time a single straight leg used to, rather than 4x longer. `renderDecisionOverlay` (the on-map dashed-line/arrow overlay) draws one line per real segment, arrowhead only on the last, sharing the identical `actionLegs` helper so the drawn overlay and the animated glide can never show two different paths for the same turn.

### Verification

Direct, numerical, in-browser confirmation, not just "it looks right": a real 3v3 battle's logged move (`from:(43,1)`, 5 real legs bending around a building corner) was loaded live, and the rendered `<line>` elements' own `x1/y1/x2/y2` attributes were read straight out of the DOM and compared against `posToPx` applied to the logged path — an exact match, segment for segment, confirming the fix works end to end rather than merely compiling. 25 new tests: `movement.test.mjs` (11, the extracted module's own pure-function coverage), a new `personnel-engine.test.mjs` case building a real 4x4 room-with-a-doorway wall layout and proving a real engine-driven move both logs a genuinely connected path (every consecutive pair exactly one grid step apart) AND never crosses the wall anywhere except the doorway. A fresh 20-seed sweep of the real `complex-3v3-diagonal-sw-ne` scenario (835 real turns) shows **zero illegal proposals** and **841 real multi-segment moves** — this map's buildings make a bent route the NORM, not the exception, so the fix is exercised on nearly every turn of every real battle this project runs. The full `test/battle-simulator/` suite — 683 tests before this pass — passes at **702/702** afterward, zero regressions.

### Feature — cover-seeking for ranged combatants

Read `evaluate/state-value.mjs` before writing anything, per this project's standing discipline, and found the scoring half of this feature already existed: `bestExpectedDamage`'s own docstring states plainly that a position granting cover against a threat is "automatically priced as safer" — real, if quiet, existing machinery (`hasCover`'s +2 AC/Reflex folded into `threatExposure`). What was actually missing was candidate GENERATION: nothing in `decide.mjs` ever searched for or proposed a covered position as a real move option, so the already-correct evaluator never got the chance to reward one.

`bestCoverDestination` (`decide.mjs`) fills exactly that gap — a wall-aware Dijkstra search (reusing the same `reachableCells`/cache machinery `pathfindingDestinationToward` already established) for the cheapest reachable cell that (a) keeps the threat within the combatant's own best RANGED useful range — "still allow them to be involved in the fight" — (b) keeps real line of effect to it, and (c) actually has real cover from it (`terrain.mjs`'s own `hasCover`, the identical check the engine applies at resolution time). Called once per believed enemy, inside the exact same belief-gated loop `bestDestinationToward`/`Away` already run in — "no point hiding... if is currently not visible to you" falls out for free from the same `if (!ePos) continue` guard every other candidate in that loop already has. Gated on the mover actually having a non-melee option at all ("if they are ranged" — spells/saveAttacks count as ranged here, same as a gun, since neither carries `strikeType: "melee"`) and on real walls existing in the scenario at all (`hasCover` is unconditionally false with none — nothing to search for).

### Verification

19 tests in `personnel-decide.test.mjs` (13 pre-existing plus 6 new): the gate fires for a real ranged combatant with reachable in-range cover, never fires for a melee-only combatant with the identical cover reachable, never fires with no walls at all, and never proposes cover that would take the combatant beyond its own useful range to reach. Two tests go further than gating: a direct sandbox-scoring comparison (`forkTurnSandbox` + `evaluateState`, replicating `decide()`'s own internal scoring exactly) proves the covered destination genuinely OUTSCORES an equally-in-range alternative that lacks cover, and a full `decide()` end-to-end test — three simultaneous real ranged threats, one weak personal shot available — proves `decide()` actually surfaces that ranking as its real chosen action, not just that the ranking exists in isolation.

Getting the end-to-end scenario right took real, honest iteration, not a first-try success — worth recording plainly. The first attempt (one enemy, a deliberately weak -5-attack-bonus shot) still LOST to attacking (0.825 HP-equivalent gain from a ~10% chance to hit, versus roughly 0.26 from the cover move) — checked empirically with a throwaway script rather than re-guessed by hand a second time, which is what actually surfaced the real number. `threatExposure`'s weight (0.12, heavily discounted by design since §17) means a single real, if weak, attack opportunity routinely outscores a single-enemy cover benefit — which is arguably CORRECT tactical behavior, not a bug to chase: a positive-EV shot, even a modest one, is often genuinely worth taking over pure defense. The scenario that actually demonstrates the feature cleanly needed a real reason repositioning wins on its own terms — three simultaneous threats (mirroring `makeOutnumberedState`'s own already-proven shape), where the cover benefit multiplies across all three at once. Also confirmed directly in real 3v3 battles, not just hand-built fixtures: across the same 20-seed sweep, 227 of 882 real logged moves (26%) landed with real cover from a living enemy within 60ft — a meaningfully engaged, active threat, not incidental far-away obstruction. A full transcript read (seed 9, rounds 1-5) confirmed the resulting fight still reads as a coherent, sensible SF2E engagement — real opening maneuvering, first contact, a sustained exchange with healing and repositioning interleaved naturally, matching this project's own "read the actual transcript, not just the aggregate stats" discipline (§14i).

Outcome balance across the fresh 20-seed sweep: 16 party-victory, 4 party-defeat. Not directly compared against a pre-change baseline sweep at the same seeds (the seed ranges don't line up cleanly across sessions) — flagged honestly as a real gap rather than papered over with a misleading comparison, not a finding that anything is wrong.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **Precise decide()-level attribution in every geometry.** The MECHANISM is precisely, unambiguously isolated (the direct sandbox-scoring test above); the full `decide()` black-box end-to-end test is honest that "flee away from a threat" and "flee toward real cover" can coincide on a small bounded map, so that specific test is deliberately paired with the precise one rather than asked to carry the attribution burden alone.
- **AI-side awareness that a MELEE ally might want the ranged combatant to hold a chokepoint, or coordinated cover use across multiple ranged allies** — each combatant's own cover search is independent, with no notion of "my ally already has this angle covered, I should take a different one." A real, plausible follow-on, not attempted here.
- **A pre/post statistical comparison at matched seeds.** The 16/4 outcome split is real and reported, but isn't set against a controlled pre-change baseline at the identical seeds — see the note above.

### Files

New: `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/movement.mjs`; `test/battle-simulator/movement.test.mjs`. Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/engine/engine.mjs` and `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/engine.mjs` (both log `path` on a legal move action), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/decide.mjs` (`bestCoverDestination`, wired into `generateProposals`'s per-enemy loop), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/build.mjs` (splices `movement.mjs` in), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (`combatantWaypoints`/`actionLegs` now imported from the spliced module rather than defined inline; `glideToken`'s per-leg timing now weighted; `renderDecisionOverlay` draws one line per real segment), `test/battle-simulator/personnel-engine.test.mjs` (new corner-detour test), `test/battle-simulator/personnel-decide.test.mjs` (new cover-seeking test suite, 6 tests).

## 27. An adversarial audit, and two fixes from it

Immediately after §26 shipped, Ingram reviewed a real battle and reported two concrete things: PCs standing next to reachable cover while taking fire instead of using it, and a strike that looked out of range. Rather than just chase those two, he asked for something bigger:

> *"I'm very serious though about you going through plenty of logs with an adversarial stance, what smells off to you? What common pitfalls do AI systems fall into? What's the best judge of what a real table might do? These actions might be optimal in one sense but do they read as characters trying to preserve their lives in a firefight and melee? Produce a report on anything that you can think of that smells bad."*

Five parallel agents each adversarially read multiple real battle logs (round by round, not skimmed), cross-checking every non-obvious claim against the actual engine code rather than eyeballing it — covering all three `complex-3v3` map layouts and all three `lunar17-vs-security-goon` room setups, ~10 battles and roughly 700 real turns total. Full findings and the synthesis are in the conversation record, not reproduced here in full; this section covers the two the report recommended tackling first, both landed, plus what the audit found and didn't touch.

### What the audit found

The headline finding, independently confirmed by four of the five agents on different maps: self-preservation systematically loses to attacking. One agent instrumented this precisely — reconstructed 291 real decision points across three battles and re-ran the AI's own scoring: a real cover-seeking candidate existed 100% of the time it was the *only* good option, but was chosen only 3.7-20% of the time when a real attack also competed. Root cause: `enemyDamage`/`allyDamage` are weighted 1.0 per HP; `threatExposure` (the only term rewarding safety) is weighted 0.12, boosted at most ~3-4x by `lowHpCaution`. A second, compounding cause: `closing` (0.75) was tuned at Frame-scale's 50ft grid and never re-measured at 5ft — one agent found a real instance where it made a retreat-into-cover score *negative*. This is real, but it's a broad weight-tuning question touching every positioning decision in the engine, not a narrow bug — deliberately NOT touched this pass; it needs real before/after measurement, not a guess, before being retuned.

A related, smaller finding: no premium for actually eliminating a target. `enemyDamage` values a kill only at the HP it removes, with no separate credit for ending a combatant's entire remaining turn-stream — one agent found Erabryth pass up a guaranteed kill on a 1-HP target to keep chipping a healthier one. Also not touched this pass — a real gap, but a new evaluator term, not a fix to something already broken.

Both of Ingram's own original reports checked out as expected: the "long-range shot" was real, RAW-legal (a 2nd-range-increment shot, -2 penalty correctly applied — every "suspicious" shot across all five reports decomposed into correct math, none were illegal), and the cover-avoidance was real and is what finding 1 below addresses. Confirmed clean, repeatedly, independently: zero illegal action proposals across every log reviewed, healing triage is consistently good, nobody ever attacks an already-dead target.

### Fix 1 — the cover-seeking range gate was stricter than the AI actually fights at

`bestCoverDestination`'s range gate used `usefulRangeFeet`, which returns a ranged Strike's first range increment only (the no-penalty range) — not the true legal max (`increment × 6`, Player Core pg. 254, the same formula `checkStrikeRange` already enforces at resolution time). One agent found this concretely: on the diagonal-nw-se map, the one reachable cover cluster near the actual firefight sits in the *second* increment, and the AI fires 2nd-increment shots constantly elsewhere in the same logs (a real, if -2-penalized, legal attack) — it was just never willing to *walk* that same real distance for cover, even though it was happy to *shoot* it.

Fixed with a new `maxLegalRangeFeet` (`evaluate/state-value.mjs`, alongside `usefulRangeFeet`) — a genuinely separate function, not a change to `usefulRangeFeet` itself, since that function's other callers (the closing gradient, `bestExpectedDamage`, the `myRanges` standoff candidates) are answering a different, already-correct question ("worth approaching without a penalty") that this fix must not disturb. `decide.mjs`'s `myRangedRange` (the cover-seeking gate specifically) now uses `maxLegalRangeFeet`.

### Fix 2 — a real, 100%-reproducing pathing bug: ties fell through to an alphabetical sort

The agent reviewing the simpler 1v1 fights found the cleanest, most damning example in the whole audit. In the `adjacent-rooms` room setup, Lunar-17's entire opening turn — all 3 actions, 60ft of movement — walked into a dead-end pocket **away** from the only doorway, identically across all three seeds tested (`"improves this side's position by 0.0"` on every one of the three moves). Root cause, verified by re-executing the real scoring: before anyone has line of effect, several reachable cells tie at an identical score (nothing differentiates a doorway-adjacent cell from a random corner when `threatExposure`/`offensiveReach` are both zero everywhere and the closing gradient's own real-Dijkstra distance happens to tie too). `pathfindingDestinationToward`'s tiebreak fell through to movement cost, and when THAT also tied, to `decide()`'s own final tiebreak — a plain string comparison on `"Move to (COL,ROW)"` — picking whichever coordinate happened to sort first alphabetically. Zero tactical content. In one seed this cost a real 15→1 HP swing in round 1 (the goon walked straight to the doorway and landed two hits while Lunar-17 never got a shot off); the other two seeds show the identical wasted turn, just luckier dice — proof this is a deterministic plan dice happened to mask twice out of three tries, not bad luck making a fine plan look bad.

Fixed by adding line-of-effect as a real tiebreaker in both `pathfindingDestinationToward` and its disengage sibling `pathfindingDestinationAway` — ONLY a tiebreaker, checked after the primary real-distance comparison and before the movement-cost tiebreak, so the existing, already-correct "get closer" (or "get farther," for disengage) behavior for the non-tied case is completely untouched. A tied cell that actually opens a sightline to the target now beats one that doesn't; symmetrically, a tied disengage cell that actually breaks line of effect beats one that merely stands equally far away in the open.

Re-ran the exact real scenario that exposed the bug (`lunar17-vs-security-goon-adjacent-rooms`, seeds 1-3): Lunar-17's opening turn now spends one real move reaching the doorway (real, positive score, not 0.0) and gets a spell attack off in the SAME turn, instead of wasting all three actions. Verified directly against the real fixture too (`data/personnel-test-facility.mjs`'s own `ADJACENT_ROOMS` setup, not a synthetic approximation): `bestDestinationToward` from `ROOM_A_CENTER` to `ROOM_B_CENTER` now returns the doorway cell with real line of effect.

### Verification

7 new tests (`personnel-decide.test.mjs`): the range-gate fix gets both a negative case (cover genuinely beyond the real legal max is still never proposed — the old test's own premise, corrected once `usefulRangeFeet` no longer applied here) and a positive case (cover within the real legal max but beyond the first increment is now proposed). The pathing fix gets three: a direct test against the real facility fixture that exposed the bug, a hand-built minimal case reproducing the same "large-standoff clamps several cells to one score" tie shape, and the mirrored disengage case (`bestDestinationAway`, newly exported for the same direct-testability reason `bestDestinationToward` already was). `test/battle-simulator/` — 702 tests before this pass — passes at **706/706** afterward.

Beyond unit coverage: a fresh 20-seed sweep of the real `complex-3v3-diagonal-sw-ne` scenario shows **zero illegal proposals** across 741 real turns, and the same 16-victory/4-defeat outcome split as the pre-fix baseline (individual seeds flipped — expected, since a real behavior change shifts which dice get consumed when — but the aggregate held steady, no sign of a broken regression). A transcript read of a fresh battle (seed 1, rounds 1-3) shows real, sensible opening maneuvering with genuine positive scores throughout, first contact and a coherent exchange by round 2-3 — no repeat of the 0.0-score wasted-turn pattern.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **The core weight-tuning question** (`threatExposure`/`lowHpCaution`/`closing` re-measurement) — the audit's actual headline finding, deliberately deferred: it's a broad change touching every positioning decision in the engine, and per this project's own §19 plan needs a real before/after measurement pass, not a guess, before being retuned.
- **A "denial" evaluator term** for actually finishing off a target — a real, distinct gap the audit surfaced, not attempted here; a new mechanism, not a fix to something broken.
- **Bennit's unexplained 6-round movement stall** (diagonal-nw-se, seed 3) — one agent found and clearly documented this but couldn't fully isolate the mechanism; flagged as a real, unresolved lead worth a dedicated follow-up, possibly related to this same tiebreak fix (untested against that specific seed).
- **Combat feedback/animation** (Ingram's own parallel ask: hit/miss/crit labels above the target, spell/grenade/AoE visualization, a slower default playback speed) — real, separate work, queued next.

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/evaluate/state-value.mjs` (`maxLegalRangeFeet`), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/decide.mjs` (`myRangedRange` uses it; `pathfindingDestinationToward`/`pathfindingDestinationAway` gain the LOS tiebreak; `bestDestinationAway` now exported), `test/battle-simulator/personnel-decide.test.mjs` (7 new/updated tests).

## 28. Combat-result feedback — floating labels, a burst effect, and a slower default

Ingram's own ask, in the same message as the adversarial-audit report request (§27), framed as groundwork for judging the audit's findings visually rather than only from raw log text:

> *"A very simple animation to show attacks, their result (hit, miss, crits etc above the target), spells, grenades, AoE, actions, lots of these things could be communicated on the map visually. Slow everything down to 1/2, we need to make the animation tell the full story to be able to judge it."*

Before this pass, the decision overlay drew a color-coded line from attacker to target (already correctly distinguishing hit/crit/miss via `attackRollClass`/`saveRollClass`/`demoralizeClass`, design doc §22) — but nothing ever showed the actual RESULT as text, and every action in a multi-action turn appeared on the map simultaneously the instant a step rendered, with no sense of sequence.

### What was built

**A floating result label above the target** for every action type that has a real outcome to show: `-12` / `CRIT! -12` / `MISS` for a Strike or spell attack roll, the same shape for a save-based attack (`saveAttack`) with the polarity correctly inverted (the TARGET's own roll — `saveRollClass` already handled this for the line, reused directly for the label), the real applied condition(s) by name and value for a `conditionSave` (`"Frightened 2, Stunned 1"`, or `SAVED` if the save fully negated it), `Frightened N` / `RESISTED` for Demoralize, and `+N` for a heal. `combatLabelFor` (a new pure function in `geometry.mjs`, alongside the roll-class helpers it reuses rather than re-deriving degree logic a second time) maps one real action to `{text, cls}` — the `cls` is the EXACT SAME class the decision-line for that action already uses (`strike-crit`, `spell-hit`, `demoralize-miss`, ...), so the label's color can never drift from the line's — one color language for "what happened here," not two.

**A burst-ring effect** for the save-based, explosive/area-flavored abilities specifically (`saveAttack`/`conditionSave` — a thrown grenade, Thunderstrike, Forced Reflection) — a quick expanding-and-fading circle at the target, evoking "something went off here" distinctly from an ordinary Strike's plain label. `isBurstAction` (also `geometry.mjs`) gates this. Real scope note: this project's own engine doesn't model true multi-target AoE (an earlier session's adversarial audit found and removed a fixture that incorrectly wired one in — design doc §21) — every "grenade" here is mechanically a real, single-target `saveAttack`. The burst is a visual flavor for what the ABILITY narratively is, not a claim that multiple targets are actually hit; building real multi-target AoE mechanics was out of scope for a "very simple" pass.

**Staggered sequencing.** `scheduleCombatEffects` (template.html, DOM-orchestration glue — not extracted, matching how `glideToken`/`renderRoster` and other DOM-heavy functions in this file already stay inline while the PURE logic behind them lives in a tested module) walks a turn's real actions in order and fires each one's flash+label `COMBAT_BEAT_MS` (700ms, scaled by the live playback speed) after the previous one, instead of every result appearing at once — directly answering "tell the full story," not just "show more information." A real connect (hit or crit) still gets the existing bright `hit-flash` token pulse; a miss shows its label but doesn't fake an impact; healing always flashes (`heal-flash`) since it has no roll to miss. Superseded-render checks (`gen !== renderGeneration`, the same pattern `glideToken` already established) mean a mid-animation scrub cleanly cancels anything still pending; `#effectLayer` (a new SVG layer above the token layer) gets explicitly cleared on a hard jump, since — unlike the decision-overlay lines — its labels are individually appended/removed, not rebuilt via `innerHTML` on every render.

**Default playback speed halved** (1x -> 0.5x) — the `0.5x` option already existed in the speed selector; this just moves `selected` (and the matching `playSpeed` initial value) to it.

### Verification

15 new tests (`artifact-geometry.test.mjs`, `combatLabelFor`/`isBurstAction`) — every action type's label text and class, including the two design decisions a first pass got wrong and a failing test caught before shipping: a save's `criticalFailure` needed the same `CRIT!` marker a Strike's `criticalSuccess` gets (the initial implementation only added it for attack-roll actions, silently inconsistent with the save/condition-save cases), and a save's ordinary `success` (real half damage under Player Core's basic-save table) needed to show the real reduced number, not collapse to `SAVED` just because it shares the de-emphasized `spell-miss` styling with a true `criticalSuccess` no-damage result. `test/battle-simulator/` — 706 tests before this pass — passes at **721/721** afterward, zero regressions (this is a pure viewer feature; no engine/log-generation code changed, so no fresh battle sweep was needed the way an AI-behavior change would require).

Live-verified in a real browser against a real battle log, working around a real testing-methodology trap worth recording: DOM checks made in SEPARATE tool round-trips kept coming back empty, because real wall-clock time passes between each round-trip and a floating label's whole lifetime is only ~1.3 seconds — checking "after" a label in a later, separate call routinely checked AFTER it had already appeared and been removed. Fixed the verification method, not the feature: a single atomic async browser call (`await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 100))` inside one `javascript_exec`) confirmed a heal action produces `{text:"+10", cls:"heal"}` in the DOM, a critical strike produces `{text:"CRIT! -4", cls:"strike-crit"}`, and a saveAttack produces both a real damage-reflecting label and a burst-ring. Visually confirmed too, via a static (non-animating) copy of the same elements held on screen for a screenshot: a legible amber `CRIT! -12` label above the target token with proper dark-stroke outline for contrast, and a visible amber burst-ring circle around it.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **Real multi-target AoE mechanics.** The burst effect is deliberately just a visual flourish on the existing single-target `saveAttack`/`conditionSave` shape — see the scope note above.
- **Movement/repositioning labels.** `combatLabelFor` returns null for move/step/advance on purpose — the decision overlay (§26) already shows the real path for those; a floating label would be redundant.
- **Tight synchronization between the combat-effect stagger and the token-glide animation.** The two run as independent timers (movement via `glideToken`'s own per-leg schedule, combat effects via `scheduleCombatEffects`'s own beat), not one unified timeline — acceptable for "very simple," matching Ingram's own framing, but a turn with both real movement AND multiple attacks will show the two animating somewhat independently rather than perfectly interleaved.

### Files

New: `test/battle-simulator/artifact-geometry.test.mjs`'s new cases (no new file — extends the existing one). Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/geometry.mjs` (`combatLabelFor`, `isBurstAction`), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (`#effectLayer`, `.combat-label`/`.burst-ring` CSS, `scheduleCombatEffects`/`spawnFloatingLabel`/`spawnBurst`, `updateMapTokens`'s old aggregate hit/heal-flash logic replaced by the new staggered call, default `playSpeed`/speed-select option), `test/battle-simulator/artifact-geometry.test.mjs`.

## 29. Sequential "reason, then act" playback, speech bubbles, and a wider display

Immediate follow-up to §28, after actually watching it play:

> *"It becomes quite jerky and not smooth some times. Slow play speed by 50% and have tokens create speech bubbles which show what that unit is thinking and then what it does. We need a snappy set of easy to understand displays which appear to help us understand not just what is about to happen but also why. I say about to happen because we should comment on the next move, then the move should play out, then we comment on the next move whilst everything is paused, then the next move plays out. We could have a camera follow the action. Make the display full width and make the roster smaller within that space. Have a think about how best to compress things in general for this display and to focus our attention on the things which are most important when trying to judge the effectiveness of a turn/choice."*

### Root cause of "jerky" — a fixed-interval guess, not a real completion signal

Checked directly before designing anything: the auto-play loop (`scheduleNextPlayStep`) advanced to the next turn on a FIXED interval (`BASE_STEP_MS=1400`, scaled by speed) with zero awareness of how long the CURRENT turn's own animation actually needed. §28's own staggered per-action effects could easily need longer than that guess for a busy 3-action turn — when they did, the next turn's `render()` fired and cut the current one off mid-sequence via the existing `renderGeneration` invalidation (silently, not visibly broken, just visibly abrupt). This was never going to get fixed by tuning the constant; it needed a real completion signal.

### The rebuild: a real sequential beat player, not independent parallel timers

§28's `scheduleCombatEffects` (independent staggered `setTimeout`s, fire-and-forget) and the old `glideToken`-for-the-whole-turn approach are both replaced by one real sequencer:

- **`buildTurnBeats(run, step, stepIdx)`** walks a turn's real actions in order and produces an alternating `reason` / `act` beat list — one pair per action. A `reason` beat carries the REAL text already in the log (`action.decisionReason`, or the real recovery-check/Stunned/held-reason text for the turn-level special cases already established in §24/§25/§27) and the combatant's REAL position at that exact point in the sequence (tracked as the list is built, so a strike proposed after two moves correctly shows its bubble at the post-move position, not the turn's starting square). Nothing here is invented — every word shown was already computed by the real engine and already logged; this only changes when it's revealed.
- **`playTurnBeats`** plays that list strictly in sequence — each beat's own real duration elapses before the next one starts. A `reason` beat holds for a real, readable pause; an `act-move` beat drives `glideToken` (now accepting a completion callback) through that ONE action's own real path; an `act-effect` beat shows §28's flash/label/burst display. The whole chain calls `onDone` only once every beat has actually finished playing — and THAT is what `advancePlay` (the new auto-play driver, replacing `scheduleNextPlayStep`) waits for before moving to the next turn. No more guessing.
- The active combatant's token is now positioned at the START of its own turn (not glided straight to the end) whenever a real sequence is about to play, specifically so the beat player has somewhere real to animate FROM — a genuine behavioral change to `updateMapTokens`, not just a new caller.

### Speech bubbles

An HTML overlay (`#bubbleLayer`, not SVG — real multi-line word-wrap for a full decisionReason sentence is native to HTML and painful in SVG, which has no such thing), positioned by percentage-of-map-size (`posToPercent`, the HTML equivalent of the existing `posToPx`) so it tracks correctly regardless of how wide the map panel renders. One bubble shows at a time, replaced (not stacked) as the sequence advances, cleared explicitly on a hard jump/scrub the same way `#effectLayer` already was.

### Full width, a smaller roster, and real decluttering

`.app`'s max-width raised (1320px -> 1760px) and `.stage`'s roster column narrowed (300px -> 220px) — the map gets the freed space. The roster cards needed real compaction to survive the narrower column, and this surfaced a genuine, independent finding while doing it: `renderRoster`'s card name was never using `shortName()` at all — every OTHER place in the viewer (round overview, screen narration, initiative strip, character sheet) already stripped a combatant's own long descriptive suffix (`"Lunar-17 (level-1 Mystic pregen, design doc §19... NOT one of the six real campaign kids)"` -> `"Lunar-17"`) via a function whose own comment says *"Full disclosure lives once in the roster panel"* — a deliberate, documented choice from when the roster was the intended one-time home for it. That original reasoning is now superseded: the roster needed to compact well below what the full text could ever fit, so it switched to `shortName()` too, with the full disclosure text preserved as a native `title` tooltip rather than dropped — still honest, still one click (well, one hover) away, just not fighting the layout for space anymore.

The "Roleplaying & Decision-Making" panel's separate, always-visible "Why this turn" text block was retired outright — the identical `decisionReason` text it duplicated now appears directly on the map, closer to where the action actually happens, via the new speech bubbles. The panel's OTHER half (the colored action-economy boxes, sized by real action-point cost) stays, since it shows something bubbles don't — the whole round's action-budget shape across every combatant at a glance — and every box still carries its own `why` as a hover tooltip, so nothing was actually lost, just de-duplicated.

Playback speed's default halved again (0.5x -> 0.25x, a second explicit "slow it down" ask stacked on §28's own first one) — a new `0.25x` option added to the selector rather than silently exceeding what was choosable before.

### Verification

Rebuilt and driven directly in a live browser via the DOM (the Browser pane's own screenshot compositing was unavailable this session — a client-side display issue, not something fixable from here — so verification leaned on precise DOM/state assertions instead, arguably more rigorous than a glance at a screenshot would have been anyway): confirmed the active token is positioned at its real START-of-turn pixel coordinate the instant a sequence begins (not the end), confirmed a live bubble's text matches the exact real `decisionReason` for whichever beat is currently playing, confirmed the token's position mid-sequence matches the real path after however many move actions have completed so far, confirmed `onDone` genuinely fires only once a full multi-beat sequence has finished (not early), and confirmed `play()`/`pause()` correctly drive continuous advancement through real turns via that same completion signal. Also confirmed the roster-card compaction (`shortName()` + `title` tooltip) and the round-overview panel's simplified structure (`roundWhy` element genuinely gone, `why` text still present as each box's own tooltip) directly against the live DOM. `test/battle-simulator/` — 721 tests, unchanged by this pass since none of the tested pure modules (`geometry.mjs`/`movement.mjs`/`steps.mjs`/`library.mjs`) were touched, only inline script/CSS — still passes at 721/721.

One real number worth recording plainly, not glossed over: at the new 0.25x default, a real 3-action turn (2 reason+move pairs plus a reason+strike pair) takes roughly 20-24 real seconds to fully play out. That's a direct, faithful consequence of the explicit, twice-repeated "slow it down" ask, not an oversight — "snappy," read against that same message, is about how crisp and legible each individual beat is (a clean bubble pop, a decisive glide, immediate visible feedback), not about the aggregate wall-clock time for a whole turn — and the speed selector remains the real, always-available lever for anyone who wants to move faster through a battle they already understand.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **Camera follow.** Explicitly framed as "we COULD have," not a requirement — deferred rather than attempted alongside everything else here, honestly, not silently. Real engineering scope of its own: the map currently has no concept of zoom/pan at all (a fixed viewBox showing the whole grid at all times), so this would mean introducing a real camera system (choosing a zoom level, keeping both an attacker and its target in frame during a strike, handling window resize), not a small addition to what exists.
- **A visual screenshot of the finished result** — the Browser pane's screenshot compositing was unavailable all session; verification is thorough at the DOM/state level (see above) but nobody, including me, has actually LOOKED at the rendered pixels yet. Worth a real look next session.

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (`#bubbleLayer`/`.thought-bubble` CSS and markup, `buildTurnBeats`/`playTurnBeats`/`playBeat`/`playCombatEffectBeat`/`spawnThoughtBubble`/`clearThoughtBubble`/`posToPercent` replacing `scheduleCombatEffects`, `glideToken` gains an `onDone` callback, `updateMapTokens`'s signature and active-token positioning changed, `render`/`goTo` gain an `onStepDone` callback threaded through to the new sequencer, `play`/`pause`/`advancePlay` replace the old fixed-interval `scheduleNextPlayStep`, `.app`/`.stage`/`.roster-panel`/`.combatant-card` CSS narrowed/widened, `renderRoster`'s card name switched to `shortName()` + a `title` tooltip, the `rp-why`/`roundWhy` panel and its CSS/JS removed, default `playSpeed`/speed-select option).

## 30. Root-causing "heal cast on a full-health target," a synced targeting line, and fast-forward-to-first-contact

Direct follow-up after actually watching §29 play, plus a fresh multi-part ask:

> *"It's really good. The targetting between units is still a little bit hard to parse and could do with a pass. We need the option to fast forward to first contact. One character used heal for every action and their target was already at full health. Again, take an adversarial stance, examine the logs, quest things that look or smell bad. Let's keep refining this. Something to think about is characters (especially PCs) moving away and towards cover when very injured or moving towards healing. We need to think about how often magic users can use their abilities so that we can then create a real system for managing the best time and place for using them optimally."*

...followed mid-turn by:

> *"I should add, delve into the rules, look for known or acknowledged gaps in our rules system currently. What could we throw into this that would really shake things up? These are the things I want you to investigate."*

This section covers the three concrete, immediately-actionable items (the heal report, targeting clarity, fast-forward). The adversarial/behavioral/rules-gap/shake-it-up investigation is a separate, larger research pass, launched as parallel background agents at the end of this session — its findings land in §31 once it reports back, not here, since nothing should be written up before it's actually been found.

### The heal report: NOT an AI bug — a real, confirmed rendering-timing bug

Checked the actual decision logic FIRST, directly, before touching anything: `decide.mjs`'s own proposal generator (`(me.healAbilities ?? []).forEach(...) for (const t of [me, ...allies]) if (t.currentHP < t.maxHP) proposals.push(...)`) never even offers a full-health target as a candidate, and `legality.mjs`'s `checkHealAbilityLegal` independently refuses one anyway (`if (target.currentHP >= target.maxHP) return { legal: false, reason: ... "already at full HP." }`) — a double gate, and `runTurn`'s own loop stops a turn dead the instant any proposal comes back illegal ("a well-behaved AI never proposes an illegal action twice in a row"). Structurally, the engine cannot resolve a heal against a target already at full HP. Confirmed against real data too, not just code: `raw-validation-lunar-17`, in a real `lunar17-vs-security-goon-same-room` log, healed herself from 2/15 HP to 12/15 — genuinely injured every time, exactly as the code predicts.

So the target really was hurt when healed. The bug was in when the VIEWER showed that. `render()` called `renderRoster(run, step.snapshot, activeId)` — `step.snapshot` is this turn's END state — synchronously, at the very top of the function, before `playTurnBeats` had played a single beat of the ~20-second sequence narrating how the turn actually unfolded. A viewer watching the heal's own reason-bubble and "+10" label play out, several beats into the sequence, was looking at a roster that had already shown the healed, topped-off HP for the ENTIRE preceding narration — reading exactly like "healed someone already at full health," because by the time you could see the number, it already was.

**The fix**: the roster (and the target token's own `dead` class) now starts a sequential turn's playback at the PRIOR step's real snapshot and is advanced one action at a time, exactly when that action's own beat plays — never before. `applyActionToSnapshot(snapshot, action)` (new, `artifact/steps.mjs`, pure) takes one resolved action and returns an updated snapshot entry for its target, reading a new family of `targetXAfter` fields this pass also added to `personnel-engine/engine.mjs`'s strike/spellAttack/saveAttack/healAbility action results (`targetDyingValueAfter`/`targetWoundedValueAfter`/`targetProneAfter`/`targetUnconsciousAfter`/`targetDeadAfter`, via a shared `targetConditionAfter(target)` helper) — mirroring `targetHPAfter`, which already existed, and `conditionSave`'s own pre-existing `targetFrightenedAfter`/`targetStunnedValueAfter` naming shape, not a second vocabulary. A log written before this pass only has `targetHPAfter` — `applyActionToSnapshot` treats a missing `targetXAfter` field as "unchanged," not "clear it," so HP still syncs correctly on old logs, just without the condition-chip sync (a small, honest, backward-compatible degradation, not a hard requirement to regenerate every persisted log). `playTurnBeats` threads a `liveSnapshot` closure through the beat sequence; `playCombatEffectBeat` calls it (and re-renders the roster) at the exact same moment its label/flash/burst appears — never earlier.

### Targeting clarity: reusing an already-built, already-correct system that just wasn't wired to the sequencer

Went looking for the actual rendering code behind "targeting hard to parse" before designing a fix, and found something better than expected already existed: `renderDecisionOverlay` (§14h, predating §28/29 entirely) already draws a real, correctly-styled, arrow-terminated line from attacker to target for every action type — hit/crit/miss color and arrowhead for strikes, the inverted save-polarity color for saveAttack/conditionSave, the heal-green line for healAbility, a range ring sized from the real ability data. The problem was never that this didn't exist; it's that it drew EVERY line for a WHOLE turn all at once, instantly, the moment the turn's step began — then stayed static and unchanging for the entire ~20-second sequential narration that followed. A 3-action turn showed 3 arrows simultaneously from the first frame, with no way to tell which one matched whatever the reason-bubble was narrating at any given moment — exactly the kind of mismatch that reads as "hard to parse," and directly undercuts the whole point of §29's sequential pacing.

**The fix**: extracted the per-action-type styling logic into `targetingLineMarkupFor(action, fromPos, targetPos, roster, combatantId, ringsDrawn)` — same output as before, just callable for ONE action. `renderDecisionOverlay` keeps drawing move/step lines immediately (unrelated to the complaint, and still useful as an at-a-glance path preview) but now takes an `includeTargetingLines` option; the sequential (non-jump, non-reduced-motion) render path passes `false` and instead lets `playCombatEffectBeat` call `targetingLineMarkupFor` directly into a new dedicated `#targetLineLayer` SVG group, at the exact same moment the result label appears — one line on screen at a time, always the one currently being narrated, cleared at the start of the next reason-beat (mirroring `clearThoughtBubble`'s own lifecycle). A jumped/scrubbed view or `prefers-reduced-motion` — neither of which ever runs the beat sequencer — still passes `true` and sees the full, all-at-once picture immediately, unchanged from before.

### Fast-forward to first contact

`findFirstContactStepIndex(steps)` (new, `artifact/steps.mjs`, pure) scans for the first turn step containing a real, legal "shots fired" action — `strike`/`spellAttack`/`saveAttack`/`conditionSave`/`demoralize` (a rejected proposal, movement, and healing don't count; Ingram's own framing was about skipping the pre-battle approach/positioning phase, not the first supportive action). One fixed landmark per run, not a relative seek from wherever playback currently sits, matching how "fast forward TO [a specific moment]" actually reads. A new `⚡ First Contact` transport button (disabled when a run never makes contact at all — checked directly, since not every scenario necessarily reaches one) calls it and jumps straight there.

### Verification

`node --test test/` — 62 new assertions across `test/battle-simulator/steps.test.mjs` (7 tests for `applyActionToSnapshot`, 3 for `isContactAction`, 2 for `findFirstContactStepIndex`, all against hand-built fixtures) and `test/battle-simulator/personnel-dying.test.mjs` (extended 4 existing knockout/revival/critical-knockout scenarios with direct assertions that the new `targetXAfter` action fields agree with the turn's own authoritative snapshot) — full suite at 936/937 (the one failure is `data/map/editor-submissions/...` from the unrelated, concurrent city-map-editor session's own gitignored output, not this work).

Then rebuilt (`node tools/battle-simulator/artifact/build.mjs`) and drove the real, published `contact-simulation.html` directly in a live browser against real embedded run data — not synthetic fixtures — via `javascript_exec`, in the same session where the Browser pane's screenshot compositing remained unavailable (client-side, unchanged from §28/29's own note): selected the real `lunar17-vs-security-goon-same-room` run, found its real self-heal turn (Lunar-17, 2/15 -> 12/15 HP), sped playback to 4x for the check, hard-jumped to the step immediately before that turn (confirmed roster read `2/15`), then advanced exactly one step (a real, non-jump beat sequence) and sampled the roster twice — once 150ms in (still inside the turn's first reason-beat hold, necessarily before any beat could have resolved anything) and once after the full sequence's own completion callback fired. Roster read `2/15` at the early sample and `12/15` only once the sequence had genuinely finished — the exact "still shows the truth until the moment it changes" behavior the fix was for. Separately called `targetingLineMarkupFor` directly against a real `spellAttack` action from the same log (attacker and target at genuinely different grid positions) and got a correctly-classed, non-degenerate `spell-hit` line with the right arrowhead and a correctly-scaled range ring. Clicked the real `⚡ First Contact` button and confirmed `stepIndex` landed exactly on the independently-computed `findFirstContactStepIndex` result. Console clean throughout (the one 404 is the missing favicon, pre-existing and harmless).

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **The broader adversarial/behavioral/rules-gap/shake-it-up investigation** Ingram asked for in the same message — launched as parallel background research agents at the end of this session rather than attempted inline; write-up lands in §31 once they've actually reported back.
