- **A real screenshot of the finished result** — still unavailable this session, same client-side limitation noted in §29. DOM/timing-level verification this pass is unusually thorough (see above), but the rendered pixels themselves remain unseen by anyone, including me.

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/engine.mjs` (`targetConditionAfter` helper, spread into strike/spellAttack/saveAttack/healAbility's return objects), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/steps.mjs` (`applyActionToSnapshot`, `isContactAction`, `findFirstContactStepIndex`), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (`#targetLineLayer` SVG group, `⚡ First Contact` button, `targetingLineMarkupFor` extracted from `renderDecisionOverlay`, `renderDecisionOverlay` gains `includeTargetingLines`, `buildTurnBeats`'s act-effect beat gains `sourcePos`, `playTurnBeats`/`playBeat`/`playCombatEffectBeat` gain a `ctx` carrying the live roster snapshot and roster-sync callback, `render`'s roster/overlay calls switched to the live/prior snapshot and `includeTargetingLines` gating), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/contact-simulation.html` (rebuilt). Tests: `test/battle-simulator/steps.test.mjs`, `test/battle-simulator/personnel-dying.test.mjs` (both extended).

## 31. Five parallel investigations: a second adversarial audit, resource-cadence data, and a grounded rules/mechanics review

§30 closed with three concrete fixes and deferred everything else in Ingram's message to a real investigation pass:

> *"Again, take an adversarial stance, examine the logs, quest things that look or smell bad. Let's keep refining this. Something to think about is characters (especially PCs) moving away and towards cover when very injured or moving towards healing. We need to think about how often magic users can use their abilities so that we can then create a real system for managing the best time and place for using them optimally."*
>
> *"I should add, delve into the rules, look for known or acknowledged gaps in our rules system currently. What could we throw into this that would really shake things up? These are the things I want you to investigate."*

Five parallel background agents, mirroring §27's own pattern: read-only, each independently cross-checking claims against real engine code and real logs rather than impressions, no files touched by any of them. All five reported back; findings below, most actionable first.

### CONFIRMED BUG: the `closing` gradient scores a free kill as a LOSS

The most serious finding of this whole pass. `evaluate/state-value.mjs`'s own header calls monotonicity ("reducing an enemy's HP must never reduce this side's score") *"the single most important structural property in this file,"* and `offensiveReach` was explicitly capped per-enemy specifically to guarantee it (§17). The `closing` term (lines 323-344) never got the same protection: it scores each ally by distance to its **nearest living** enemy. Kill that nearest enemy — even one already well within weapon range, contributing nothing to the term — and the search jumps to whatever enemy is next, often much farther away, and the ally is retroactively penalized for "not closing" on a fight a teammate just finished.

Proven two ways: a minimal isolated case (2 allies, 2 enemies, realistic distances, called directly against the live `evaluateState`) where killing an already-in-range 1-HP enemy drops the party's own score by **10.6 points** (-21.894 alive vs. -32.500 dead); and a real instance reconstructed from `complex-3v3-diagonal-sw-ne--seed-2.json` round 6, where a Strike against a 1-HP enemy at clear range and line of effect — an almost-certain kill — scores **-4.86**, so the AI takes a worse-scoring action instead (a 2-action spell against an already-unconscious ally, see below). Neither the Frame-scale nor personnel-scale monotonicity test suite catches this: both keep every test combatant clustered within trivial range in their shared fixture helper, so `closing` is permanently zero in every existing test — real zero coverage, not a near-miss.

Mechanistically distinct from `threatExposure` (safe by construction — a sum over visible enemies, so removing one can only shrink it) and from §27's already-known "no denial premium" gap (that's a missing bonus; this is an active, wrong-direction penalty).

### CONFIRMED BUG: Demoralize never checks whether the target can perceive the demoralizer

`legality.mjs`'s `checkDemoralizeLegal` checks actor-alive, budget, target-not-dead, range, the ATTACKER's own perception, and line of effect — never the target's. Player Core pg. 200 is explicit: *"If the target can't understand you or can't hear you, Demoralize doesn't work"* — and an unconscious combatant can't perceive anything at all. Real impact: across the full 33-log sample, Demoralize is used only 4 times total, and **3 of those 4 (75%)** target a combatant independently confirmed unconscious at that exact moment. The one legitimate use is the only one RAW would actually allow.

### REAL WEAKNESS: the evaluator is completely blind to `dyingValue`/`woundedValue`

`evaluateState` never references either field (confirmed by grep) — only `stunnedValue` is priced. Once a combatant hits 0 HP, every HP-keyed term floors out identically whether they're freshly unconscious or one hit from permanent death, so there's no mechanism rewarding "finish this dying enemy before they recover" or "protect my dying ally." Concretely, in the same `seed-2` battle: an enemy casts its full 2-action spell at an already-unconscious ally, twice, in consecutive rounds, scoring a flat 0.0 both times — mechanically indistinguishable from doing nothing — and even the actual killing blow that later ends that PC's life for good scores exactly 0.0 too. Distinct from §27's "no denial premium" (a missing offensive bonus) — this is a whole state axis §25 added specifically to make death matter, invisible to the AI reasoning about it.

### Confirmed: the §27 LOS-tiebreak fix helped, but didn't fully close its own flagged lead

§27 left "Bennit's unexplained 6-round movement stall" (`complex-3v3-diagonal-nw-se--seed-3`) as an open lead, untested against the LOS-tiebreak fix. Re-run live against current code (the stored log for that file predates the fix by ~3 hours, so it was re-run rather than trusted): the stall shrank from 5-6 wasted rounds to about 1.3 — the fix helps once a LOS-granting cell is reachable, but ties among equally-blind cells still fall through to the alphabetical tiebreak below that threshold. Side finding worth acting on directly: 4 of the 33 in-scope log files are stale (generated ~3 hours before the fix landed) and sit in the log store with nothing marking them as such — a real risk that a future audit re-flags already-fixed behavior as new.

### Corroboration, not new: real numbers for §27's already-deferred weight-tuning question

Demoralize's near-total disuse (4 times in 525 multi-strike turns) traces to the same underweighted `threatExposure` (0.12) §27 already root-caused. A more vivid instance of the same low-HP-doesn't-retreat pattern the injured-PC investigation (below) independently found in bulk: `bennit-p2` at 3/15 HP, having just taken a 12-damage crit, attacks again on its very next action rather than retreating further.

### Checked and cleared — recorded so they aren't re-flagged

Ingram's own illustrative example, "Demoralize on an already-maximally-frightened target," never actually happens — zero instances across all 33 logs (the real Demoralize problem is the unconscious-target gap above, not over-stacking). No wall-clipping or impossible-position strikes anywhere (independently re-verified LOS/range/reach for every targeted action in the sample against the real engine functions). General movement "thrashing" is basically resolved — once a naive revisit-scan was corrected to require a genuine departure-and-return (camping a good firing position for several rounds isn't thrashing), only 4 minor 2-3-tile detours remain across the whole sample. Flanking, noted in §25 as not yet observed firing, now fires correctly at least twice in current data, exactly as that section predicted it eventually would.

### Injured-PC self-preservation: confirms §27's finding again, independently, and finds a genuinely new gap

Directly investigating Ingram's "moving away and towards cover when very injured, or moving towards healing": low-HP caution DOES exist (`lowHpCaution: 2.0` scales `threatExposure`'s cost as HP drops, capping at 3x at 0 HP) and cover-seeking candidate generation DOES exist (§26) — but across 39 real turns where a party member dropped to ≤34% max HP, 72% show zero movement at all, and **0% show a pure retreat** — every turn that moved also attacked, and even a character sitting at 1/15 HP for four consecutive rounds only ever retreated toward genuine, engine-confirmed cover as a leftover action after already attacking. This independently reproduces §27's own measured 3.7-20% cover-seeking win rate against a competing attack, from a completely different angle (bulk low-HP-turn sampling rather than reconstructed decision points).

The genuinely new finding: **"move toward my own healer because I'm hurt" does not exist anywhere in the code.** The only ally-directed movement candidate in `decide.mjs` is gated on the *ally's* HP (a healer approaches a hurt teammate) — never on the mover's own HP or need. 54% of the low-HP sample is one specific, different, working pattern instead: Lunar-17 standing in place and self-healing, even at 1/15 HP, since her heal ability targets herself directly rather than needing to close distance to anyone.

Grounded sketch for later (not implemented): the natural fix for the retreat-losing-to-attack side is either a non-linear `lowHpCaution` curve (steep only in the last ~20% of HP, rather than today's strictly linear ramp) or a separate, non-discounted "imminent-death" term reusing the existing `bestExpectedDamage` estimator. The missing healer-seeking candidate could mirror the existing hurt-ally-directed movement loop directly — but would need a companion evaluator term (structurally parallel to `offensiveReach`) pricing "is an ally's heal currently in reach from here," since `decide()`'s single-ply lookahead can't otherwise credit a move toward help that doesn't pay off until next turn.

### Spellcaster resource cadence: real numbers, as requested, not a system yet

`decide.mjs` has zero resource-specific logic of any kind. The only mechanism anywhere is a flat `resource: 3.0` weight in `state-value.mjs` — +3.0 HP-equivalent per remaining use, applied identically whether it's the 1st or the last charge, with no reference to round number or how much of the fight remains, and applied only to the evaluating side's own allies (never modeling "deplete their resources" either).

A finding the question's own framing didn't anticipate: across the entire roster, only **one** ability anywhere carries a finite `usesRemaining` — Bennit's Thunderstrike (3 charges). Everything else, including both of Lunar-17's own abilities, is unlimited, which is why she self-heals as liberally as a cantrip (including topping off from 87-93% HP when nothing better-scoring is available) — there's nothing there to conserve.

Bennit's Thunderstrike is therefore the one real test case, and the real pattern is a clear **nova**, not hoarding: 91% of all possible charges get used, 73% of battles spend all 3, 0% of battles bank 2-3, and first use often lands in round 1-2. Of the minority of battles ending with one charge unspent, the large majority are explained by the battle (or Bennit) ending before another turn arrived — not one clean case anywhere in the sample of the AI visibly holding a charge in reserve because the fight looked nearly over.

### Rules-gap sweep

A structural gap, not just one missing fact: GM Core's complete by-level numeric bands (AC, HP, Saves, Perception, Strike attack bonus, Spell DC, Hazard Defenses/Offense) sit fully captured in the SRD mirror but were never extracted into `reference/tables/`, unlike the two tables that were. This is the same category of gap that already caused a real, previously-documented bug (the archetype battery's party fixture came out under-armored relative to these same bands, caught only by hand-checking line by line) — worth closing directly, not just noting.

The known Elite/Weak gap is now pinpointed further: the mirrored article cuts off mid-sentence ("as noted") right where the real delta table almost certainly continues on the live page — a known re-fetch target with a specific URL now, not just an open question.

A small, easy correction: `reference/tables/dc-and-check-math.json`'s `resistanceWeaknessRangeByLevel` field cites Alien Core and carries an unresolved "cross-check before relying on this" hedge; the real source is GM Core pg. 123, and the extracted numbers already match it exactly — just the citation needs fixing, not the data.

A process catch worth taking seriously: Athletics maneuvers (Grapple/Shove/Trip/Disarm/Reposition) were deliberately deferred once, narrowly, for the very first Lunar-17-vs-Security-Goon matchup specifically — then silently dropped from every subsequent "what wasn't attempted this pass" list across §23 through §30, unlike genuinely comparable gaps (Tumble Through, greater/lesser cover, true multi-target AoE) that WERE properly carried forward as tracked scope every time. It simply fell off the list rather than being re-affirmed as intentionally out of scope — worth a real decision before the roster grows further or the six real kids/Frames (who will normally want to grapple/trip/shove/disarm as ordinary SF2E characters) get built.

Two small, low-priority mirror-completeness issues also turned up: `reference/srd/alien-core/1274-appendix.json` has real metadata but a completely empty `text` field (a genuine capture stub), and `reference/srd/gm-core/997-perception.json` ends in a truncated, bled-together fragment from the next article (harmless — the real table it's missing exists complete elsewhere and was independently confirmed to match).

What checked out clean, worth recording so it isn't re-audited: `dcByLevel` matches its cited source exactly across the full level range; the encounter/XP party-size scaling matches GM Core's own formula presentation; all 17 individual skill articles are genuinely present (Phase 0's skill-mirroring plan was actually completed, despite what older framing elsewhere might suggest); a broad hedge-language sweep of every schema file found nothing beyond the one already-known Elite/Weak note; `campaign-arc.md` and the Claimant guidelines are clean.

### Grounded "shake it up" proposals, prioritized

Baseline confirmed directly from the code: 8 action types total (move/step/strike/spellAttack/saveAttack/demoralize/conditionSave/healAbility), terrain is wall-geometry only (no difficult terrain, no hazards, no elevation, no per-cell movement cost anywhere in `pathfind.mjs`), only 5 of SF2E's ~40 real conditions are modeled, cover is pure passive geometry with no action ever spent on it, and perception is deliberately binary (a documented choice, made after a real oscillation bug — see below).

1. **Athletics maneuvers (Trip/Shove/Grapple/Disarm/Reposition)** — richest payoff, and not competing with anything: it's the direct, now-unblocked continuation of `ideas/entries/raw-sf2e-encounter-validation.json`'s own already-in-progress next step, and closes the rules-gap sweep's process-catch finding above. The first REAL control actions — spending an action to degrade what an enemy can do next turn, rather than only ever spending it on damage. Demoralize's own resolution shape (check vs. a real DC, per-degree outcome table) is already structurally almost identical to what Trip needs. Genuinely new work: Shove/Reposition need code that can move a combatant OTHER than the acting one (today only the actor's own position is ever written), and Grabbed/Restrained is the first *relational* condition (tied to who's holding whom, not a flat scalar), needing a new Escape proposal type.
2. **Take Cover (a real action)** — smallest lift of anything proposed. Upgrades today's always-on, action-free standard cover to +4 AC/Reflex/Stealth for a turn spent not attacking, reusing the exact `hasCover`/`coverBonus` arithmetic every attack resolution site already computes. The first genuine "give up this exchange's offense for real safety" tradeoff.
3. **Difficult/hazardous terrain** — real, cited GM Core numbers (extra movement cost; hazardous terrain deals real dice damage on entry), but honestly the most invasive of the movement-adjacent proposals: the per-square movement cost is currently a hardcoded constant in four separate places (`pathfind.mjs`'s Dijkstra flood plus three of `decide.mjs`'s own destination-search helpers), all of which would need to become terrain-aware together.
4. **Complex hazards as scripted "extra combatants"** (e.g. a sentry turret with its own initiative, HP/Hardness/Broken Threshold, and a fixed action routine) — SF2E's real mechanism for "reinforcements," and cheaper than it sounds: `engine.mjs`'s turn loop is already fully generic over what a "combatant" is, so a hazard that rolls initiative and runs a canned action list per round is structurally close to just another roster entry.
5. **Detection states (Hidden/Undetected)** — real and well-documented (a flat check before an attack roll even matters), but flagged deliberately last and high-risk: building it means reopening `vision.mjs`'s binary perceive/don't-perceive model and `belief.mjs`'s narrow, position-only memory, both of which are documented, DELIBERATE simplifications adopted specifically after a real evaluator-oscillation bug from unreliable position data (§15q) — not oversights waiting to be filled in.

Explicitly investigated and declined, on the record so they aren't re-proposed: called shots (searched the full mirror — this isn't a real PF2e/SF2e-family mechanic; Strike is a single undifferentiated roll); a numeric morale/rout threshold (the Fleeing condition itself is real, but SF2E has no numeric morale table anywhere in the mirror — any specific trigger number would be this project's own invention, same category as the already-flagged Elite/Weak gap, and GM Core's own guidance says to leave a mass-flee moment to GM judgment outside normal turn resolution entirely); Recall Knowledge (real, but wouldn't create a new decision for THIS specific AI, which already has perfect sandbox information about enemy stats — already tracked as its own idea for a different, GM-facing purpose).

### What's next (not decided here)

Two items above are confirmed, isolated, proven bugs rather than judgment calls — the `closing`-gradient monotonicity violation and Demoralize's missing target-perception check — and read as the natural next "start here" candidates, matching how §27's own confirmed findings got picked up directly. Given the evaluator's own header names monotonicity "the single most important structural property in this file," a change there deserves the same explicit go-ahead every other priority shift this session has had, not an assumption. Left for Ingram's call, along with which (if any) of the injured-PC/resource-cadence/rules-gap/shake-it-up threads to pick up next.

### Files

None — this entire section was read-only investigation across all five agents; no repo files were modified by any of them.

## 32. Acting on it: two confirmed §31 bugs fixed, two fresh reports, and an explicit AI-behavior policy

Direct green light on the §31 findings, plus two new reports from actually watching §30's fix and two explicit design decisions:

> *"I noticed a heal being cast through a wall. A heal was cast on someone dying and their hit points went [up] but the dying indicator didn't disappear straight away. Speech bubble needs to be smart about it's positioning, not overlapping with tokens, x2 larger and on the actual speech bubble at the bottom should be the name of the action being taken followed by the symbols that SF2E uses for actions. The idea is to link the action to the cost of that action. Make sure all names are the real names of those things. Why would someone demoralise a downed target anyways? That's completely illogical? Infact the only reason to target a downed unconscious character would be to heal them. NPCs normally 'die' when they get downed, they don't usually progress through the stages of dying or at least most GMs would never bother to simulate that. A note about killing, we will not actually build an AI which kills downed players deliberately. That is often considered to be something to reserve for extreme situations as it makes killing a character so much easier to accidentally do. However, player character will naturally want to kill nearly dead nearby baddies. Fix that too."*

### Heal through a wall — confirmed, and the ONLY action-legality check missing Line of Effect

Checked directly: `checkHealAbilityLegal` never accepted a `walls` parameter at all, unlike every sibling check in the same file (`checkStrikeLegal`/`checkSpellAttackLegal`/`checkSaveAttackLegal`/`checkDemoralizeLegal`/`checkConditionSaveLegal` all independently gate on `lineOfEffect`). Not a subtle bug — a straightforward omission, confirmed by direct comparison rather than assumed. Fixed by adding the parameter and the identical `lineOfEffect` check every other action already has, and threading `state.walls` through the one real call site in `engine.mjs`. Two new tests in `personnel-legality.test.mjs`: a heal blocked by a wall between healer and target, and a heal that stays legal when walls exist elsewhere on the map but don't block this particular shot.

### The dying indicator lag — §30's fix was correct; the persisted logs just hadn't caught up to it yet

Traced directly through `conditions.mjs` (`applyRevival` correctly zeroes `dyingValue`; `isUnconscious`/`isDead` are both derived, never stored, so there's no way for them to drift out of sync with `currentHP`/`dyingValue`) and confirmed the existing `personnel-dying.test.mjs` revival test already asserted the engine gets this right. The actual cause: every one of the 60 runs embedded in the viewer — all 33 personnel-scale ones included — was generated BEFORE §30 added the `targetDyingValueAfter`/`targetUnconsciousAfter`/etc. fields to strike/spellAttack/saveAttack/healAbility's return objects (`logs/index.json`'s own `generatedAt` timestamps: 2026-08-23, hours before that fix landed). `applyActionToSnapshot`'s graceful-degradation path (an old log missing a `targetXAfter` field leaves that field as it was, rather than clobbering it with `undefined`) was working exactly as designed — but that meant EVERY currently-viewable heal was silently taking the degraded path, HP syncing correctly while the condition chips stayed frozen at whatever they'd been at turn-start, which is precisely "hit points went up but the dying indicator didn't disappear straight away."

**Fix: regenerate the data, not the code.** Re-ran `run-personnel-scenario.mjs` for all three room configurations of both scenario families (all 33 personnel-scale logs), then rebuilt the artifact. Verified directly against the fresh data: found a real revival case (`complex-3v3-diagonal-sw-ne` seed 9, Tapkey healed from 0 HP/dying 1/unconscious back to positive HP), drove it live in the browser exactly as in §30's own verification — roster read "0/14, Unconscious — Dying 1" both immediately before the turn AND 150ms into its playback (still inside the first reason-beat hold, necessarily before the heal's own beat could have resolved anything), then correctly cleared to a plain HP reading with no chip once the full sequence finished. The lag is gone with real data behind it, not just in theory.

### "Why would someone demoralise a downed target?" — confirmed as §31's Priority 2, fixed as an AI-policy choice, not a rules change

Ingram's question is the same bug §31 already found (3 of 4 real Demoralize uses in that audit's sample targeted an unconscious ally) restated from first principles, plus a broader instruction: *"the only reason to target a downed unconscious character would be to heal them,"* and separately, *"we will not actually build an AI which kills downed players deliberately... player characters will naturally want to kill nearly dead nearby baddies. Fix that too."*

Important distinction, checked before writing any code: Player Core does NOT forbid attacking an unconscious creature — §25's own research already established this (an unconscious PC stays a real, legal Strike target, off-guard and all, until dying 4). So this isn't a legality bug to fix in `legality.mjs` — it's a deliberate choice about what this project's AI will ever attempt, and it belongs at the decision layer, not the rules layer. Fixed in `decide.mjs`: a new `targetableEnemies = enemies.filter((e) => !isUnconscious(e))`, used by the five offense/social candidate loops (strike, spellAttack, saveAttack, demoralize, conditionSave) in place of the unfiltered `enemies` list. `enemies` itself stays unconscious-inclusive for movement/positioning, and healing deliberately still targets `allies`/`me` directly, unfiltered — an unconscious ally is exactly who should be healed. Three new tests in `personnel-decide.test.mjs`: a full-kit baseline proving all five proposal types normally fire against a conscious target, the same fixture proving all five vanish once that target is unconscious, and a confirmation that healAbility is unaffected.

Verified against the freshly-regenerated real data: searched every action in all 60 runs, cross-referencing each target's state at the moment of the action — **zero instances** of strike/spellAttack/saveAttack/demoralize/conditionSave ever targeting an unconscious combatant, down from §31's measured 3-of-4 Demoralize violations. Total Demoralize usage also dropped (4 uses in the old 33-log sample -> 2 in the fresh regeneration) — consistent with the illegitimate uses simply no longer existing, not with Demoralize becoming broken.

On the NPC-death half of the same message — *"NPCs normally 'die' when they get downed, they don't usually progress through the stages of dying"* — checked directly and this is already exactly how the engine works, and already correct RAW (Player Core pg. 402: the dying/unconscious spiral is explicitly a PC-and-significant-NPC rule, not universal). `conditions.mjs`'s `isDead` returns true for any non-`party` combatant at 0 HP unconditionally, before ever consulting `dyingValue` — an ordinary NPC has no unconscious state to be in at all. Nothing to fix here; a confirmation, not a bug.

### The `closing` gradient — fixed exactly as §31 found it, with a formal proof this time

§31's Priority 1: killing an already-in-range enemy could make the party's own score DROP, because the closing-gradient's "distance to nearest LIVING enemy" search would jump to a farther surviving enemy the instant the near one died. Root cause, confirmed by reading `evaluateState`'s own top (`const all = [...state.combatants.values()]` — no HP filter at all; `enemies`/`allies` include dead combatants from the start) alongside the closing loop's own `if (enemy.currentHP <= 0) continue;`, which was the ONE place actually excluding them from this specific search.

**The fix is a one-line removal**, not a reweight: stop excluding dead enemies from the nearest-distance search. This is provably safe, not just empirically better: `state.positions` never changes when a combatant dies (only move actions touch it — confirmed via `engine.mjs`'s own `livingPositions` comment, which already documents that dead combatants' positions persist deliberately, for the viewer's own benefit). So a dead enemy's entry in the search is IDENTICAL — same id, same frozen position — in the state immediately before and immediately after their own death. The set being minimized over never changes size or values across exactly the transition this bug cared about, so `nearest` (and therefore the whole term) can only ever stay EXACTLY the same when an enemy dies, never jump. A genuinely closer LIVING enemy is still correctly preferred over a farther corpse, same as before — only the "reassign to someone farther away" discontinuity is gone.

Two new tests in `personnel-state-value.test.mjs`, deliberately placing an enemy OUT of range (every pre-existing monotonicity test in this file clusters everyone within a few squares, so `closing` was permanently 0 and silently untested by any of them — confirmed directly, not assumed): one proving killing an already-in-range enemy while a farther one survives no longer lowers the score, one proving the term still does real work (an ally out of range of everything still scores worse than one already in range) so the fix didn't accidentally gut the mechanism §22 built this term to provide.

### Speech bubble: bigger, collision-aware, and shows real action cost

Three changes to `template.html`, all in `buildTurnBeats`/`spawnThoughtBubble`/new helpers, none touching the underlying beat-sequencer architecture from §29/§30:

- **2x larger** — every CSS dimension doubled exactly (200px->400px max-width, 12px->24px font, etc.), not eyeballed.
- **Real action name + real SF2E action-cost icons**, at the bottom of the bubble, separated from the reasoning text by a rule. `realActionName(action)` returns the genuine Basic Action name (Stride/Step/Strike/Demoralize — Player Core pg. 408/410) for action types that don't already carry their own specific name, and the real extracted ability name (`spellName`/`abilityName`/`saveAttackName`/`conditionSaveName` — e.g. "Eldritch Lance," "Transfer Vitality") for the rest, unambiguously the real name of the actual thing happening rather than a guess at a generic verb category. `actionCostIcons(n)` renders Player Core's own Action Icon Key (383-actions.json: Single Action / Two-Action Activity / Three-Action Activity / Reaction / Free Action) as repeated ◆ glyphs (◇ for a free action, ↺ for a reaction — neither currently produced by this engine, kept for completeness) — reading `action.actionsCost`, a field every action type already carried.
- **Collision-aware positioning** — `pickBubblePlacement` tries four placements in order (above/below/right/left of the speaking token) against a conservative estimated bounding box, picking the first that doesn't come within a token-radius of any OTHER living combatant's real current position (`step.snapshot`, since only the acting combatant moves during their own turn — no need for the beat sequencer's own live-updating snapshot here). Falls back to the original above-token default if all four collide with something. Each placement bundles its own CSS transform and tail direction so the two can't drift out of sync with each other.

### Verification

`node --test test/battle-simulator/` — 738/738 (up from 715 before this pass' new tests: 2 heal-wall, 2 closing-gradient, 3 unconscious-targeting). Then, in order: regenerated all 33 personnel-scale logs against the fixed code, rebuilt the artifact, and drove the real, freshly-regenerated data live in the browser — the same rigor §30 established, now proving the fixes against real battles rather than only hand-built fixtures: zero unconscious-target violations across all 60 embedded runs (down from 3-of-4 Demoralize violations measured in §31), a real revival case's condition chip confirmed staying accurate through the exact pre-heal window it used to lag in, the action-footer confirmed showing real names ("Stride," "Eldritch Lance," "Transfer Vitality," etc.) with correctly-repeated action-cost glyphs, and the collision-avoidance confirmed actually engaging (a live-captured bubble landed at `place-right`, and a direct unit check confirmed a token placed directly above an anchor correctly pushes the choice to `place-bottom`). Console clean throughout (the one 404 is the missing favicon, unchanged from every prior pass).

One honest data point from the regeneration, not chased further: `complex-3v3-diagonal-sw-ne` seed 15 now times out at the 40-round limit (previously a 9-round party-victory under the old, buggy code) — `illegalProposals: 0` and `utilization: 1` throughout, so this reads as a real, legally-fought, unlucky/cautious battle rather than a stall or a new bug, but the full transcript wasn't read end-to-end to confirm that qualitatively the way a genuinely suspicious result would warrant.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **§31's Priority 3** (the evaluator's blindness to `dyingValue`/`woundedValue`) — a real, softer weakness, not a confirmed bug with a proof like the two fixed here, and genuinely a weight/incentive question rather than a one-line correction. Left for a real before/after measurement pass, matching this project's own repeated caution about evaluator changes.
- **Everything else from §31** (injured-PC-toward-healer, resource-cadence system, rules-gap sweep items, the shake-it-up menu) — none of it was asked for by name this turn; "fix everything you can" was read as scoped to this message's own concrete reports plus the two already-confirmed, already-isolated §31 bugs, not as silent authorization to build new features or retune weights speculatively.
- **A real screenshot** — still unavailable this session, unchanged from every prior pass' note.

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/legality.mjs` (`checkHealAbilityLegal` gains `walls`/Line of Effect), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/engine.mjs` (the one call site now passes `state.walls`), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/decide.mjs` (`targetableEnemies`, `isUnconscious` import, five proposal loops switched to it), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/evaluate/state-value.mjs` (closing-gradient's dead-enemy exclusion removed), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (`.thought-bubble` CSS doubled and split into 4 placement variants, `realActionName`/`actionCostIcons`/`bubbleBoxFor`/`pickBubblePlacement` added, `buildTurnBeats` computes `otherPositions`/`actionFooter` per reason beat, `spawnThoughtBubble` takes and renders both), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/contact-simulation.html` (rebuilt), all 33 personnel-scale files under `tools/battle-simulator/logs/` plus `logs/index.json` (regenerated). Tests: `test/battle-simulator/personnel-legality.test.mjs`, `test/battle-simulator/personnel-decide.test.mjs`, `test/battle-simulator/personnel-state-value.test.mjs` (all extended).

## 33. Speech bubble: a fixed corner and a real tail, replacing §32's dodge-the-nearest-token scheme

Direct follow-up after actually watching §32's positioning fix in play:

> *"Speech bubbles are often off screen, often overlapping things. Move the bubble itself into a corner with a long tail to the unit thinking it."*

### Why §32's fix wasn't enough

§32's `pickBubblePlacement` tried four placements (above/below/left/right of the speaking token) and picked whichever didn't collide with another token's estimated position — but it only ever checked for OTHER TOKENS, never the map's own edges. A combatant near a map boundary has nowhere clean to dodge into regardless of which of the four directions gets tried, so the bubble could still render partly or fully off the visible map. The fix isn't a fifth placement or a smarter collision box — it's not anchoring to the token's position at all.

### The fix: one fixed corner, one real tail line

`.thought-bubble` now has a single, constant CSS position (`top: 3%; left: 3%` — the top-left corner of the map; checked directly that nothing else in `.map-wrap` competes for that space, since `#scenarioNote` and the initiative strip are separate panels outside the map, not overlays on top of it) — on-screen by construction, for any token anywhere on any map, at any zoom. `drawBubbleTail(tokenPos)` (new, `template.html`) draws the connection instead: a real SVG `<line>` from a fixed point near the bubble's own corner (`bubbleCornerPx()` + a fixed offset estimate — not a measured DOM rect, since the bubble's real rendered size depends on wrapped text length that isn't known until after layout, and a tail reads fine even a few pixels short of the literal edge) to the token's actual `posToPx` position, plus a small dot marking exactly who's speaking. Dashed rather than solid, specifically so it reads as "a connector" and never gets confused with the solid `.decision-line` targeting lines from §28/§30. Drawn into a new `#bubbleTailLayer` SVG group (between `#tokenLayer` and `#effectLayer`, so the tail stays visible crossing over other tokens but sits under any combat-result label). `clearThoughtBubble` now clears both the bubble div and the tail layer together, so the two can never fall out of sync with each other.

The whole four-placement collision system from §32 (`bubbleBoxFor`, `pickBubblePlacement`, the `otherPositions` computation in `buildTurnBeats`, the four `place-*` CSS variants and their four directional keyframes) is deleted outright rather than left dormant — it solved a narrower problem than the real one and the real one doesn't need it.

### Verification

Rebuilt and driven live against real, freshly-regenerated battle data: picked a turn whose acting combatant sits near the BOTTOM-left of a 44x30 map (far from the bubble's fixed top-left corner — a genuine stress case, not a convenient one) and confirmed directly via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the bubble's rendered box stays fully within the map SVG's own rendered rectangle on all four edges. Confirmed the tail line's own endpoints match exactly: the bubble-side start at the expected fixed corner offset, and the token-side end at the exact same pixel coordinates `posToPx` independently computes for that combatant's real position (75, 825) — not approximately, exactly. Confirmed the tail layer never accumulates stale elements across multiple reason beats (always exactly 2 children — one line, one dot — matching the clear-then-redraw lifecycle). Confirmed `pickBubblePlacement` is genuinely gone, not just unused. Console clean (the one 404 is the missing favicon, unchanged from every prior pass).

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (`.thought-bubble` CSS simplified to one fixed position, `.bubble-tail-line`/`.bubble-tail-dot` CSS added, `#bubbleTailLayer` SVG group added, `bubbleCornerPx`/`drawBubbleTail`/`BUBBLE_CORNER_PCT`/`BUBBLE_TAIL_START_OFFSET` added, `bubbleBoxFor`/`pickBubblePlacement`/`posToPercent` and the `otherPositions` plumbing through `buildTurnBeats`/`spawnThoughtBubble` all removed, `clearThoughtBubble` now also clears the tail layer), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/contact-simulation.html` (rebuilt).

## 34. Forced Reflection: a real, if latent, RAW-fidelity bug — caught by Ingram's own quoted rule text

> *"Pretty sure I saw this spell be used on someone twice, that's not legal! [pasted the real Forced Reflection stat block, highlighting] Regardless of the result, the target is then temporarily immune to Forced Reflection for 1 day."*

### What the data actually showed

Checked directly, both ways, before touching any code: scanned all 33 personnel-scale logs on disk for (a) the SAME attacker using `conditionSave` against the SAME target+ability twice, and (b) ANY target hit by `conditionSave` more than once regardless of attacker. Zero instances of either, across 36 total legal `conditionSave` actions. The mechanism was, and had been, working correctly for every battle actually on disk.

### The real, if currently invisible, bug

The exclusion was tracked as `attacker.conditionSaveTargets` — a per-ATTACKER "have I tried this on them" list, the identical shape Demoralize's own `demoralizedTargets` uses. That shape is correct for Demoralize (Player Core: *"YOU can't Demoralize the same creature again"* — explicitly attacker-scoped) but wrong for Forced Reflection, whose real text Ingram quoted is phrased from the TARGET's side: *"the target is then temporarily immune to Forced Reflection."* Immune to the ability, not to one specific creature's use of it. With only one Forced-Reflection-capable creature in the entire current roster (Erabryth — confirmed by checking every fixture in `raw-validation-fixtures/`), this distinction is currently unobservable: attacker-scoped and target-scoped tracking produce identical results when there's only ever one possible attacker. It stops being invisible the moment a second one exists — which is exactly why Ingram's own careful reading of the rule text, not the log data, is what caught it.

### The fix

Moved the tracking from `attacker.conditionSaveTargets` (a `"name|targetId"` string list) to `target.conditionSaveImmunities` (a plain list of ability names, now living on the TARGET). `legality.mjs`'s `checkConditionSaveLegal`, `decide.mjs`'s own proposal filter, and `engine.mjs`'s write-back and sandbox-clone-forward all updated together. Demoralize's own, genuinely-different, attacker-scoped `demoralizedTargets` mechanism is untouched — the two abilities' real text actually differs, so their code correctly differs too, rather than sharing a single mechanism that was only ever accidentally right for one of them.

### Verification

Seven new tests across `personnel-legality.test.mjs` (rewrote the two existing tests to set immunity on the target rather than the attacker, added a new test proving a SECOND, different attacker is also blocked once a target has already saved against an ability — the exact case a single-attacker roster can never expose) and `personnel-decide.test.mjs` (the same target-side check at the proposal-generation layer, plus a control proving immunity to one ability doesn't block a different one). Full suite green. One further check, done directly rather than assumed: a pre-existing end-to-end integration test (`personnel-engine.test.mjs`) that scripts the same attacker repeatedly trying `conditionSave` on the same target was still asserting the OLD rejection wording ("already tried") in its regex match — a real, if narrow, second casualty of the fix, caught by running the full suite rather than just the files touched directly. Updated to match the new, more accurate wording ("already immune"); the underlying behavior (attempt 1 legal, every later attempt rejected) is unchanged and was never what broke.

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/legality.mjs`, `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/engine.mjs`, `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/decide.mjs`. Tests: `test/battle-simulator/personnel-legality.test.mjs`, `test/battle-simulator/personnel-decide.test.mjs`, `test/battle-simulator/personnel-engine.test.mjs` (all extended/corrected).

## 35. Real cover-seeking, an aggression dial, and a genuinely blind randomized opening

The largest single ask this session, three related threads from one message:

> *"I also note that tokens still don't run for cover, they don't seek to stand next to cover and place it between themselves and their opponents. Once they get within range they just plunk away with ranged weapons and everything stops being a simulation and ends up looking very strange. Could we introduce some behaviours? Maybe we could have some characters act more aggressive, moving towards enemies."*
>
> *"We shouldn't always spawn our two sides in the same places, we should spawn them randomly within two randomly chosen arcs with a distance between each other. If a token starts being able to see an opponent token then that's not a valid starting point. That means we get a moment of tokens exploring, where they move around almost randomly, then once contact begins characters move towards their nearest enemy. Eventually we might make it that good and bad characters alike move towards the sounds of violence... The idea is that we need to do this to actually get a wide range of results and start getting better feedback on what's working and what isn't."*

### Cover-seeking: real, measured before/after, not a guess

§31's own audit had already root-caused why nobody retreats: `lowHpCaution`'s linear ramp capped out at a mere 3x even at 0 HP (weight 2.0), and `threatExposure`'s own base weight (0.12) is so heavily discounted that even that ceiling (0.36 effective) never got remotely close to `enemyDamage`/`allyDamage`'s 1.0-per-HP scale — a real, mediocre attack routinely outscored full retreat regardless of how close to death anyone was. Ingram watching real play independently confirmed the exact same symptom from the outside, unprompted.

Two changes to `state-value.mjs`'s `lowHpFactor`, together: `lowHpCaution` raised 2.0 -> 8.0 (bringing the ceiling to 9x, ~1.08 effective — just past parity with a real attack), and the ramp changed from linear to CUBIC in `(1 - hpFraction)` — deliberately still continuous, no threshold/cliff (§22's own "moving 3x is an alarm" lesson), just shaped so the higher ceiling only actually bites in roughly the last quarter of HP rather than making moderately-hurt combatants over-cautious through the whole middle of the range.

**Measured, not guessed**, using a fresh, purpose-built script (`measure-low-hp-retreat.mjs`, scratchpad-only) applying §31's own methodology — every party-combatant turn starting at <=34% max HP, did the turn's first move increase or decrease distance to the nearest living enemy — run once against the logs on disk BEFORE regenerating (60 logs, old weights) and once after (75 logs — see below for the +15 — new weights):

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Qualifying low-HP turns | 97 | 126 |
| Zero movement | 36.1% | 27.0% |
| Moved AWAY (real retreat) | 33.0% | **42.9%** |

A real, directional, measured improvement — not a total transformation (this is one weight change, not a rewrite of the tactical layer), and stated honestly as such.

### `aggression`: an opt-in per-combatant dial, not a new taxonomy

Ingram's "maybe some characters act more aggressive" is answered with the smallest mechanism that does the job: a single optional numeric field, `combatant.aggression` (default 1 — every existing fixture completely unaffected), read inside `evaluateState`'s own per-ally loop and applied as a divisor on that ONE combatant's caution factor (`cautionFactor = lowHpFactor * outnumberedFactor / aggression`). A value above 1 makes a combatant discount their own exposure risk — "moving towards enemies" falls out as an emergent consequence of reduced fear, not a second, independent "wants to attack" mechanism layered on top. Deliberately NOT wired onto any real fixture yet — which specific character (if any) should be the aggressive one is a roster/creative call, not an engineering one, and nothing in Ingram's message named a specific character; the mechanism is real, tested, and ready whenever that call gets made.

### The exploration gap this whole feature exposed

Building genuinely blind deployment surfaced a real, necessary companion problem: `generateProposals`'s entire movement-candidate section is driven by `believedPositions` (design doc §15s) — with zero known enemies, EVERY enemy-directed destination candidate is skipped, and with no hurt ally or scripted objective either, `destinations` stays completely empty. A combatant in that state would hold forever, every turn, since nobody can ever discover anybody if nobody moves. A new, gated candidate fixes this: when `believedPositions.size === 0`, propose one real, seeded-random reachable cell (via `reachableCells`, using `decisionRng` — never the battle's own dice stream, matching this file's own established separation) as a genuine "explore" move. This is what makes "a moment of tokens exploring, moving around almost randomly" a real, working behavior rather than just a description of intent.

### Genuine fog of war at deployment — a real, scoped fork of an existing decision

`belief.mjs`'s `refreshBelief` has always seeded every side's belief with the TRUE enemy lineup at deployment, regardless of Line of Effect — a deliberate §15s decision ("everyone sees the battlefield lineup before wall quirks kick in"), reasonable for the existing hand-picked setups where "the lineup" and "a specific room" were the same known thing. It's fundamentally incompatible with what Ingram is now asking for: a genuine "find each other first" opening requires actually not knowing where the enemy started. Rather than silently overriding a real, working, deliberate prior decision for every scenario, `refreshBelief` now checks a new opt-in `state.blindDeployment` flag — set ONLY by the new randomized-arc deployment (below) — and starts belief genuinely empty when it's true. Every existing fixed setup keeps §15s's original behavior completely unchanged; this is a new, narrower mode sitting alongside it, not a replacement.

### Randomized arc deployment, with a real, verified guarantee

New, map-agnostic module (`scenarios/random-deployment.mjs`): pick two directions from the map's center at least 90° apart (a real angular-separation floor, not just "random and hope"), walk each out to near the map's own boundary (a real ray-to-box-edge intersection), cluster each side's combatants there in the same tight-arrival-together shape the existing hand-picked setups already use, and — the actual point — reject the whole attempt and re-roll (still fully seeded/reproducible) if ANY combatant on one side would start with real Line of Effect to ANY combatant on the other. Wired into `personnel-complex-battle.mjs` as a new `"RANDOM_ARCS"` setup value, which also sets the new `blindDeployment` flag on the returned scenario — the deployment guarantee and the belief fork are meant to be used together, and `RANDOM_ARCS` is what turns both on at once. A new named scenario, `complex-3v3-random-arcs`, registers it for real log generation alongside the existing fixed setups (round limit raised 40 -> 60: a genuinely blind opening spends real turns exploring before contact is even possible, on top of whatever the fight itself needs afterward).

### Verification

24 new tests: `random-deployment.test.mjs` (ray-box geometry, cluster placement including a real wall-sealed-neighbor case, and the deployment generator itself — zero cross-side Line of Effect on a real dividing-wall map, in-bounds/no-overlap, exact reproducibility per seed, real variety across seeds, the 90° separation floor checked structurally across 20 seeds, and a deliberately-impossible zero-wall map proving it throws a clear error rather than silently violating its own guarantee), plus new `personnel-complex-battle.test.mjs` coverage proving the same guarantee holds on the REAL complex-battle map (not just the generator's own synthetic test map), reproducibility/variety at the full scenario-builder level, clean end-to-end resolution across 5 seeds, and — the actual behavioral proof — a real battle showing genuine pre-contact movement with zero frozen turns.

Then generated 15 fresh `complex-3v3-random-arcs` logs and rebuilt the artifact (75 runs total now, up from 60), and drove one live in the browser: deployment placed the two sides at (28,28)-(29,29) and (1,17)-(2,18) on the 44x30 map — genuinely far apart, not a corner-adjacency accident — first contact landed at step 63 of 100, and every single one of the 62 pre-contact turns showed real movement (0 frozen turns) — the exploration behavior working exactly as designed, not just in theory. Confirmed the existing "fast-forward to first contact" button (§30) still lands exactly on the independently-computed contact step against this new deployment mode too. Console clean throughout.

The full battle-simulator suite required three real fix passes to go green after this pass's changes — each one a genuine, distinct casualty of a deliberate behavior change, not a flaky retry: two stale test assertions (§34's own "already tried" -> "already immune" wording change, and this section's `lowHpCaution` linear -> cubic curve, both updated to assert the new intended shape rather than the old one) and one registry-completeness test that hardcoded the exact set of six scenario names and needed the new seventh added. All caught by running the FULL suite, not just the files touched directly — the personnel-engine.test.mjs and run-personnel-scenario-cli.test.mjs casualties in particular lived in files this pass never directly edited.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **§31's Priority 3** (the evaluator's blindness to `dyingValue`/`woundedValue`) — still untouched, still flagged, still a separate weight/incentive question from the concrete fixes above.
- **"Move towards the sounds of violence"** — Ingram's own framing was explicitly "eventually," not now. Would need combat NOISE as a real, positional, decaying signal distinct from belief's current position-only memory — real future scope, not attempted here.
- **Assigning `aggression` to any specific real fixture** — the mechanism is built and tested; which character (if any) becomes the aggressive one is left for Ingram's own call, not decided here.
- **A real screenshot** — still unavailable this session, unchanged from every prior pass' note.

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/evaluate/state-value.mjs` (`lowHpFactor` cubic + raised ceiling, `aggression` dampener), `tools/battle-simulator/ai/knowledge/belief.mjs` and `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/knowledge/belief.mjs` (both — real separate copies, not a re-export — gain the `blindDeployment` fork), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/engine.mjs` (`state.blindDeployment` wiring), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/decide.mjs` (the blind-exploration candidate), `tools/battle-simulator/scenarios/personnel-complex-battle.mjs` (`RANDOM_ARCS` setup), `tools/battle-simulator/run-personnel-scenario.mjs` (`complex-3v3-random-arcs` registered). New: `tools/battle-simulator/scenarios/random-deployment.mjs`. Regenerated: all 33 pre-existing personnel-scale logs plus 15 new `complex-3v3-random-arcs` logs, `logs/index.json`, `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/contact-simulation.html`. Tests: `test/battle-simulator/personnel-state-value.test.mjs` (extended), `test/battle-simulator/random-deployment.test.mjs` (new), `test/battle-simulator/personnel-complex-battle.test.mjs` (extended), `test/battle-simulator/run-personnel-scenario-cli.test.mjs` (corrected).

## 36. Directed exploration ("vector toward center of mass," stick together), and live LOS rendering

> *"We need to rethink what PCs do when there is no visible enemy. They need to explore in a way that sends them in the vector of the center of mass of their opponent and try to stick together as a group. At the moment they move randomly which means it takes ages for them to get line of sight of each other."*
>
> *"It would be be nice if we rendered the actual LOS of the tokens when it's their turn so that you can see what that token can see, highlighting tokens by making them full brightness only when actually visible, the rest of the time make them muted."*

### Directed exploration

§35's own blind-exploration candidate picked a uniformly random reachable cell every turn — real movement (fixing the freeze bug), but with no sense of direction at all, so two sides genuinely could and did wander for a long time before either one got lucky. Two candidates now, not one:

1. **A blended target** — a real point along the line from this side's own center of mass toward the TRUE center of mass of the living opposing side, weighted 55%-75% toward the opponent (jittered per decision, not fixed) so the party visibly advances rather than just orbiting its own start. Using true opposition positions here isn't a new kind of omniscience introduced for this feature — `state-value.mjs`'s own `closing` gradient already scores movement against true enemy positions unconditionally on belief (see that file's own header), so this candidate is the movement-*generation* side of the identical, already-accepted split this project has had since §17: true position can inform movement, real perception still gates every actual attack. Blending in the party's OWN centroid (not aiming purely at the enemy) is the direct answer to "try to stick together as a group" — every member's blended target converges on roughly the same point, so the party advances as a loose formation rather than each combatant computing an independent beeline that could scatter them.
2. **A genuinely random reachable cell**, kept from §35, so a blocked or currently-unreachable blended point never leaves a combatant with only one brittle option.

**Measured, not assumed**, using `findFirstContactStepIndex` (already built for the fast-forward button, §30) against the 15 `complex-3v3-random-arcs` logs, regenerated once before this change and once after:

| | Before (pure random) | After (directed) |
|---|---|---|
| Average turns before first contact | 20.5 | **6.7** |
| Pre-contact turns as a share of the whole battle | 31.6% | **13.8%** |

Roughly a 3x reduction in exploration time — directly answering "takes ages." A live spot-check also confirmed real cohesion in practice: at the midpoint of one battle's exploration phase, the three party members' maximum pairwise distance was under 8 squares — a real formation, not three individuals racing off independently.

Building this required one genuinely important, hard-won test-methodology lesson, worth recording honestly: an early version of the new tests used wall-less fixtures (`walls: []`) for simplicity, and every one of them silently passed while testing the WRONG code path. `blindDeployment: true` only stops belief from being seeded with the true lineup at the very FIRST refresh — it does nothing to stop that same refresh's own real-time perception check from immediately re-discovering an enemy anyway, if literally nothing blocks the sightline (`lineOfEffect` with no walls is unconditionally true). The fix wasn't in the exploration code at all — it was giving the test fixtures a real dividing wall, matching this project's own established convention (`random-deployment.test.mjs`'s `centerDividerWalls`), so `believedPositions` genuinely stays empty and the new code actually gets exercised.

### Live LOS rendering

A real, parallel port of `engine/terrain.mjs`'s own `lineOfEffect`/`wallBlocksSight`/`segmentsIntersect` into `geometry.mjs` (`lineOfEffectLocal` — the artifact has no module loader once published, so it can't import the engine tree directly, the same reasoning `reachFeetForStrikeLocal` already established) — cross-checked in the test suite against the real engine function across hand-built cases AND a real spread of position pairs on the actual complex-battle map (opposite corners: blocked; same room: clear; the one real courtyard-door sightline design doc §24 built on purpose: clear; across an internal wall: blocked), so this can never silently drift from what the AI itself actually reasoned about.

`updateTokenVisibility(run, step, activeId, activePos)` toggles a new `.not-visible` CSS class (`opacity: 0.4`, deliberately excluded from a `.dead` token — a corpse isn't "hidden," it's a different fact) on every OTHER token based on real Line of Effect from the ACTIVE combatant's own current position — never the true omniscient board state, exactly the acting token's own real, current view. Called at the start of every beat (reason/act-move/act-effect) using that beat's own tracked position, called again once an act-move beat's glide actually finishes (so visibility reflects where the token really ended up, not just where it started), and called once more at the top of every `render()` for the jump/scrub/reduced-motion/no-beats cases that never reach a beat at all. Verified live against a real battle: every token's dimmed/bright state matched `lineOfEffectLocal`'s own independent computation exactly, for every combatant, at a real mid-battle turn.

### Verification

10 new tests: 5 in `personnel-decide.test.mjs` (closer-to-true-opponent bias, real group cohesion via the ally-centroid blend, jitter-driven variety across 8 decision indices — the one that caught the wall-less-fixture bug above — and graceful behavior with zero living enemies), 6 in `artifact-geometry.test.mjs` (`lineOfEffectLocal` cross-checked against the real engine on open ground, a real blocking wall, a sight-permitting window, an irrelevant wall, the real complex-battle map across 4 position pairs, and the tip-touching-doesn't-block edge case). Full suite green throughout.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **Regenerating the 6 fixed-setup scenario families** — untouched by this pass; their belief is never empty (§15s's own default seeds it immediately), so the new exploration code structurally never runs for them. Only the 15 `complex-3v3-random-arcs` logs needed fresh data.
- **"Move towards the sounds of violence"** — still explicitly "eventually" per Ingram's own §35 framing, still not attempted.

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/decide.mjs` (`averagePosition`, the blended+random exploration candidates), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/geometry.mjs` (`lineOfEffectLocal`/`segmentsIntersectLocal` and their private helpers), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (`.token.not-visible` CSS, `updateTokenVisibility`, wired into `playBeat`'s three branches and `render`'s own baseline pass), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/contact-simulation.html` (rebuilt). Regenerated: the 15 `complex-3v3-random-arcs` logs, `logs/index.json`. Tests: `test/battle-simulator/personnel-decide.test.mjs`, `test/battle-simulator/artifact-geometry.test.mjs` (both extended).

## 37. Merged-move playback, no pausing before contact, and the real bug behind wall-hugging oscillation

> *"If all a character does is move multiple times we only need to include a single 'move' over all, that should speed things up a lot. Hell, make it so that we don't have any pausing until first contact, once you hit play tokens just move towards each other in turn, only once a token can see another token do we slow things down into an explaining mode."*
>
> *"I still see examples of PC tokens which are moving backwards and forwards, probably because they are next to a long wall and can't play multiple turns ahead. They need to be able to plan multiple turns ahead so that they can move around very large objects without thinking they are stuck."*

Three asks, one viewer-pacing pair and one real AI bug.

### Merged-move playback

`buildTurnBeats` used to give every single action its own reason-then-act beat pair, even when a turn was nothing but three or four consecutive Strides — three or four full "here's why, then watch it move" cycles for what reads, visually, as one continuous walk. New `groupTurnActions` (`movement.mjs`, tested independently rather than left inline — this project's own extraction convention for real logic living inside `template.html`, matching `geometry.mjs`/`steps.mjs`/`library.mjs`) collapses any run of CONSECUTIVE legal move/step/advance actions into a single `moveGroup` — one reason beat, one glide, every real leg from every action in the run concatenated in order. A non-move action (or a rejected proposal) still breaks the run and gets its own beat, unmerged — a Stride-Stride-Strike turn still reads as move, then a real separate strike moment, not one bubble covering an attack it didn't explain.

### No pausing before contact

`isPreContact` (`stepIdx < findFirstContactStepIndex(steps)`, recomputed per turn — the array is small, and this keeps `buildTurnBeats` a pure function of its own three arguments) now suppresses the reason bubble entirely for a pre-contact move group: no bubble, no `REASON_HOLD_MS` wait, straight into the glide. The glide itself still plays at the normal speed-adjusted pace, so motion stays visible — it just never stops to narrate a decision that, during blind exploration, is always the same generic "keep advancing" choice. The turn that actually CONTAINS the first contact action gets full explaining-mode treatment (not just turns strictly after it) — `isPreContact` is false exactly at `firstContactIdx`, so the attack that finally breaks the silence gets narrated, not just its aftermath.

Verified directly against a real `complex-3v3-random-arcs` run rather than assumed: patched `buildTurnBeats` in the live artifact to log every call, then played a real battle through. Confirmed 12 straight pre-contact turns (11 real combatant turns before the fight starts) produced beat sequences of exactly `["act-move"]` each — zero reason beats — and the very next turn, at the step `findFirstContactStepIndex` itself names as first contact, produced `["reason", "act-move"]` immediately followed by a real strike turn's `["reason", "act-effect", "reason", "act-effect", "reason", "act-effect"]`. Explaining mode starts exactly where it should, not one turn early or late.

(One test-methodology note worth recording: real-time verification via the browser preview pane was blocked for most of this check by the pane reporting `document.hidden === true` even after being explicitly fronted — a known limitation this session already has for screenshot compositing, now confirmed to also throttle/suspend the CSS-transition-driven token glide entirely, matching `glideToken`'s own decade-old code comment about hidden tabs suspending rAF. Worked around by temporarily monkey-patching `glideToken` in the live page to resolve instantly instead of via real CSS timing — that made the underlying sequencing logic race through cleanly, proving the STRUCTURE is correct independent of the browser tooling's rendering limitation. The real, unpatched code is untouched; this was a verification workaround only, not a change to the shipped behavior.)

### The real bug behind "moving backwards and forwards near a long wall"

Ingram's own diagnosis — "can't play multiple turns ahead" — pointed at the pathfinding horizon, but `bestDestinationToward`'s `trueDistanceField` (§16) already floods a real Dijkstra distance field from the target across the WHOLE map (a generous `span`, not bounded to one turn's Speed) — it was already multi-turn-aware in exactly the sense needed. The actual bug was smaller and stranger: §36's blended exploration candidate computes its target as a raw arithmetic blend of two centroids —

```js
const blended = {
  col: allyCentroid.col + (enemyCentroid.col - allyCentroid.col) * towardEnemyWeight,
  row: allyCentroid.row + (enemyCentroid.row - allyCentroid.row) * towardEnemyWeight
};
```

— a **float**, not a grid cell. `reachableCells`'s Dijkstra flood generates every neighbor by adding ±1/0 to the flood origin's own col/row; flooded from a non-integer origin, EVERY key it ever produces is non-integer too ("33.7,0", never "34,0"). The mover's own reachable-this-turn cells are always real integer grid cells, so `trueDist(col, row)`'s lookup into that field NEVER matched, EVERY time, for this one candidate — silently falling through to its own fallback, `distanceFeet(...) * 10`, a pure straight-line estimate with zero wall-awareness. That's the whole bug: the wall-aware multi-turn field was already built and already correct, but the one candidate that needed it most was handing it a key it could structurally never look up, so it always got the myopic straight-line answer instead — walk straight at the wall, get blocked, walk straight at the wall again next turn from a slightly different angle, repeat.

Fixed by rounding the blend to the nearest real cell before searching:

```js
const blended = {
  col: Math.round(allyCentroid.col + (enemyCentroid.col - allyCentroid.col) * towardEnemyWeight),
  row: Math.round(allyCentroid.row + (enemyCentroid.row - allyCentroid.row) * towardEnemyWeight)
};
```

No new pathfinding machinery required — the multi-turn-aware search this whole feature always needed was already there.

**Proven with a real regression test, not just reasoned about**: a full-height dividing wall with a single gap at rows 18-22, mover and enemy centroid both on row 0 on opposite sides of the SOLID part of the wall. Confirmed by temporarily reverting just the rounding fix (`git stash push` on `decide.mjs` alone, new test left in place) that the un-rounded candidate stays at row 1-2 — hugging the wall it can't get through — while the fixed version correctly biases toward the gap (row > 2). Both runs captured in `test/battle-simulator/personnel-decide.test.mjs`; only the fixed version is what ships.

**Measured, not assumed, on the real scenario this bug was hit in**: the 15 `complex-3v3-random-arcs` logs, regenerated with the fix.

| | Before (unrounded blend) | After (rounded) |
|---|---|---|
| Round-limit timeouts | **1 / 15** (seed 6, hit the full 60-round ceiling) | **0 / 15** |
| Average rounds (excluding the timeout outlier) | 7.1 | 6.8 |

The timeout is the headline result: seed 6 never resolved within 60 rounds under the old code — exactly the "stuck, can't get past the wall" failure mode Ingram was watching happen — and resolves in 9 rounds with the fix. Every other seed's round count stayed in the same tight range, confirming this wasn't a broader rebalance, just the one dead-lock case going away.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

- **Regenerating the 6 fixed-setup scenario families** — their belief is never empty, so the exploration candidate this bug lived in structurally never runs for them; only `complex-3v3-random-arcs` needed fresh data.
- **A genuine multi-turn lookahead search** — considered and explicitly NOT built. The evidence pointed at a real but narrow bug in an existing, already-adequate multi-turn-aware field, not a missing capability — building new planning machinery on top of a broken lookup would have masked the actual defect rather than fixed it.

### Files

Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/movement.mjs` (`groupTurnActions`), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/template.html` (`buildTurnBeats` rewritten around `groupTurnActions` + `isPreContact`, `playBeat`'s act-move branch reads `beat.legs` directly), `tools/battle-simulator/personnel-engine/decide.mjs` (blended exploration target rounded to a real cell), `tools/battle-simulator/artifact/contact-simulation.html` (rebuilt). Regenerated: the 15 `complex-3v3-random-arcs` logs, `logs/index.json`. Tests: `test/battle-simulator/movement.test.mjs` (7 new `groupTurnActions` cases), `test/battle-simulator/personnel-decide.test.mjs` (1 new wall-detour regression case). Full suite: 780/780.

## 38. A fourth PC, a fourth NPC, real area-burst damage, and a real 4v4 — "will your simulations reveal any group failures?"

> *"Pick a new pregen - add it - pick a new NPC - add it. Apparently 4 level 1 PCs should actually potentially struggle against 4 NPCs at level 1. I wonder if your simulations will reveal any group failures. When you add a PC or NPC I'd like you to note any gaps that haven't been filled yet in terms of what they can do and the simulation, pick something and fill that gap with functionality."*

Four real deliverables from one message: a fourth PC, a fourth NPC, one real gap closed with real engine functionality, and a real 4v4 to see whether the roster's own honest struggle-or-stomp question actually holds up.

### Choosing the fourth PC and NPC: re-verified against real data, not re-trusted from an old survey

§21's own research survey named 5 remaining Player Core pregens and a shortlist of bestiary NPCs — re-checked directly against the real pack data this session rather than assumed still accurate:

- **Jaedana (Operative)**: still throws — `toIngredients()` hits an ambiguous ability boost with no recorded resolution, confirmed by actually running it.
- **Old-Growth Wildfire (Soldier)**: still has nothing usable — her only two weapons are a real area-burst grenade (out of scope on the PC side, see below) and a Machine Gun whose raw `weapons` entry has no melee fallback item on her sheet at all; Auto-Fire is a separately out-of-scope multi-target category.
- **Vorza Moonshot (Solarian)** — the pick: `toIngredients()` runs clean, and her real, achievable kit is one ranged Strike (Zero Pistol, 1d6 cold, derives through the existing `deriveStrikes` unmodified) plus Demoralize. Her own distinctive gap: a Solarian's whole class fantasy — a Solar Weapon, manifested only while attuned (Solar Manifestations) — has **no static item representation on her sheet at all**, confirmed directly (her raw `weapons` list is just Electromag Grenade + Zero Pistol, nothing else) — unlike a grenade or a sidearm, there's no physical fallback item to extract a melee Strike from. Not an extraction shortfall; a real property of her real kit.

On the NPC side, re-scanned ALL 23 real level-1 NPCs across both bestiary packs directly (not just the 4 the old survey had named), screening each for a genuine single-target `system.action:"strike"` melee/ranged item:

- **Tough (`rulebook-bestiaries`)** — the pick, matching §21's own old finding exactly: only Gun Bash (`system.action:"strike"`, +9, 1d4+4 bludgeoning) is a real Strike; her other two weapons are both real but out of scope — Scattergun is `"auto-fire"` (a cone, multi-target, the same category as Old-Growth Wildfire's Machine Gun), and her **Frag Grenade is a real `system.action:"area-fire"` burst** — exactly the mechanism this pass needed to build anyway.

### The gap: every grenade this project has ever extracted was flagged "not wired"

Lunar-17's Incendiary Grenade (§19), Tapkey's own copy (§21), Bennit's Frag Grenade (§22), now Vorza's Electromag Grenade — four separate PCs, four separate honest notes saying the same thing: *"wiring a burst weapon through saveAttack would have hit exactly one creature with none of the real friendly-fire risk an area weapon actually carries."* That's the gap this pass closes — not a new PC-side derivation, a genuinely new ENGINE mechanism: a real multi-target basic-save burst.

**`personnel-engine/legality.mjs`** — `checkAreaAttackLegal`: unlike every other save-based action, there's no single `target`, only an aim point (`originPos`) and a radius. Range/budget/perception/LOS all check against that point; a new `affectedCount` param (computed by the caller from real state, since legality.mjs's other functions never scan the whole combatant map) rejects an aim point that would catch nothing — a real grenade is never worth throwing at empty ground.

**`personnel-engine/engine.mjs`** — a new `"areaAttack"` proposal type, in both outcome tables. Real PF2e/SF2e area damage is rolled **once** and applied to every caught creature according to THEIR OWN save degree — not a fresh roll per target — so `REAL_OUTCOMES.areaAttack` rolls the shared damage first, then resolves each affected creature's basic save against it. `EXPECTED_OUTCOMES.areaAttack` calls the already-existing `expectedSaveDamage` once per affected creature (no new math needed there at all). "Who's caught in the blast" is computed from real `state.positions`/`state.combatants` — every LIVING combatant within `radius` feet of the origin, **either side, attacker included** if they're close enough to their own throw. Cover is judged from the blast origin, not the attacker (once it lands, what matters is what's between the explosion and each creature it's near).

**The friendly-fire pricing needed zero new code in the evaluator.** `evaluateState` (state-value.mjs) already reads every combatant's live `currentHP` straight off the sandboxed state for its `enemyDamage`/`allyDamage` terms — never from a per-action result object. Because the new `areaAttack` resolution genuinely mutates HP on every affected creature, an origin that would also catch an ally scores exactly as badly as any other ally-damaging proposal, automatically. Proven end to end, not just reasoned about: a decide() test with two equally-reachable enemy targets, only one of which stands next to an ally, correctly and consistently picks the clean one.

**`pack-extractor/extract-npc.mjs`** — `deriveAreaAttack(npc, {name, saveType, actions})`, the extraction half. Structurally read from the real item: burst radius (`system.area.value`), throw range (`system.range.increment`), damage. The DC needed a real, cited convention rather than a guess: the attack-row item carries no separately stored DC field at all (confirmed by reading its complete raw JSON) — Player Core's own `@Check[reflex|against:class|basic]` phrasing plus the standard "10 + attack bonus" NPC shortcut this system already uses elsewhere gives DC 17 for Tough (10 + her real 7). `usesRemaining` comes from the real carried quantity on the paired `weapon`-type item (a thrown grenade is a grenade you don't have anymore). Two structural guards (`system.action !== "area-fire"`, `area.type !== "burst"`) stop a future caller from silently mis-wiring a plain Strike or a cone/line effect as a burst.

**What this deliberately does NOT reach**: a **PC's own** area weapon needs its DC from their Class DC (`10 + class DC proficiency + key ability modifier`) — a real derivation this project has never built (only Spell DC exists, via `deriveSpellDC`). Vorza's Electromag Grenade and every earlier PC's own grenade stay unwired for exactly this reason, now stated precisely in their fixtures' own notes rather than just "not wired." Scattergun/Machine Gun-style Auto-Fire (a real, different multi-target shape — a cone/line, not a burst) stays out of scope, matching every prior Auto-Fire note in this project.

### The real 4v4: Lunar-17+Tapkey+Bennit+Vorza Moonshot vs. Security Goon+Xenowarden Greenhorn+Erabryth+Tough

Registered as `complex-4v4-random-arcs` — every real fixture this project has built, one side each, `RANDOM_ARCS` deployment (§35) so no fixed setup's own starting geometry could bias the result either way. Verified structurally first (LOS-blind deployment scales cleanly to 4v4, zero illegal actions across many seeds, all 8 combatants present, a real spread of outcomes) — then run for real, 30 seeds, to actually answer the question asked:

| Outcome | Count |
|---|---|
| Party victory | 15 |
| Party defeat | 13 |
| Timeout (60-round ceiling) | 2 |

**Yes — the simulation genuinely does reveal group failures.** 13 of 30 real fights (43%) end in a full party defeat, not a curiosity or an edge case. This is the roster's first real, symmetric matchup — every earlier scenario in this project (2v2, 3v3) skewed heavily toward party victory — and a roughly coin-flip result across 30 independently-seeded fights is exactly the kind of finding Ingram's own question was actually asking for: proof that "4 PCs vs. 4 NPCs at level 1 is a genuinely dangerous fight," not an assumption.

A real trace confirms the new mechanism firing correctly in an actual AI-chosen battle, not just in isolated tests: in seed 8 (a party defeat), round 2, Tough throws her Frag Grenade at Bennit's square (best of 11 legal options that turn, +6.8 net score) — 1d8 rolls a 5, Bennit makes her save and takes half (2 damage, to 13/15), Lunar-17 (standing 5ft away) fails hers and takes the full 5 (to 10/15). One thrown grenade, two PCs actually hit — real friendly-fire-shaped multi-target damage, playing out exactly as designed, in a battle nobody scripted.

### Verification
