This isn't a bug: `setupValue` (§42) exists exactly to price "an ally who hasn't acted yet can capitalize on this," and both combatants here have real, hard-hitting flat-bonus melee weapons (Gun Bash 1d4+4, Nano-edge Rapier 1d6+3) that the default roster's own security-goon/lunar-17 pairing doesn't really have an equivalent of. Whether `baseline-v1`'s CURRENT weights price this combo correctly on a roster where it's this decisive is exactly what the overnight search below tests.

### The overnight run

Two chained weight searches (`weight-search.mjs`, unchanged, single-key mode — the same tool §49's own 507-generation run used), targeting the two rosters with REAL, measured losses rather than merely-unexplored ones:

- Chain A (360 min budget): `tough.json`+`xenowarden-greenhorn.json`, excluding `objective`/`resource`/`tempoDenial` (all three remain genuinely inert on this roster too — neither fixture carries a limited-use item or a Stunned-inflicting ability).
- Chain B (180 min budget, auto-chained after Chain A via `&&`): `obozaya.json`+`kuriaku.json`, same exclusions.

Launched detached (`nohup ... & disown`, matching §49's own proven survive-a-restart pattern), logging to `tools/battle-simulator/logs/overnight-chainA-*.log`/`overnight-chainB-*.log`, checkpointing exactly as `weight-search.mjs` already does — each chain's own final checkpoint additionally copied to a roster-specific filename before the next chain starts, since the checkpoint path itself isn't per-roster, so a resume of Chain A specifically (if ever needed) doesn't get silently overwritten by Chain B's own progress.

### What wasn't attempted this pass

A deep overnight search on `bennit`+`erabryth` — deprioritized on purpose: that roster showed no real losses, so exploring its two newly-unlocked weight dimensions (`resource`, `tempoDenial`) is lower urgency than fixing two roster/archetype pairings where `baseline-v1` is CURRENTLY losing. Real fixture diversity beyond the 10 already in `raw-validation-fixtures/` — out of scope, not needed to find this finding. Any change to `evaluateState`/`decide()` itself in response to the Trip-pile-on mechanism — deliberately left for the search (and, if it fails to find anything, a follow-up investigation) rather than hand-tuned on a hunch from one transcript.

### Files

New: `tools/battle-simulator/analysis/roster-generalization-sweep.mjs`, `test/battle-simulator/roster-generalization-sweep.test.mjs`. Changed: `tools/battle-simulator/analysis/metrics.mjs` (dying-turn idle-rate fix), `test/battle-simulator/metrics.test.mjs` (regression test). Real n=24 sweep results and the overnight search's own reports append to `battle-history.md` as they land — never hand-edited. Full suite re-run clean after the metrics.mjs fix.

## 55. pileOnRisk — pricing the diagnosed loss directly, and why it is a stopgap

The overnight run (§54) finished: Chain A (tough+xenowarden-greenhorn) ran 3,352 generations and promoted nothing — never once beating more than 3 of 6 archetypes, a clean structural negative. Chain B (obozaya+kuriaku) "promoted" 36 candidates in ~136 generations; the first (a ×1.0037 mutation) and the latest (34 mutations deep) both lost to `baseline-v1` at an identical 45.8% at n=24, against a 52.1% baseline-vs-itself control at n=48 — the whole chain was noise. Root cause, now recorded as a real limit of the tool: the promotion bar is only hard to clear by luck when the champion is already dominant against its pool; near parity, noise clears it constantly. (Chain B also ended on an uncaught `visibleEnemyDamages is not defined` — a self-inflicted race: the file was being edited on disk while the still-running search kept spawning fresh worker threads that import it. Its 136 completed generations ran on the old, correct code.)

Direct fix for the diagnosed mechanism: a new `evaluateState` term, `pileOnRisk` (`state-value.mjs`, weight 0.25, NOT YET MEASURED like `closing`/`setupValue`/`mobilityDenial`). For each of my own not-yet-off-guard combatants, if a visible enemy with real Athletics access is adjacent, it prices the *extra* expected damage every *other* visible enemy would deal if that maneuver landed — weighted by the maneuver's real success probability (`degreeProbabilities`, the identical Trip/Grapple DC formula `outcomes.trip`/`outcomes.grapple` use: Athletics vs 10 + Fortitude) and by the ally's existing caution factor. Symmetric and monotonicity-safe by construction (same `currentHP <= 0` gate as `threatExposure`); exactly zero in any 1-enemy-per-side fight, which is why `ai-profiles.test.mjs`'s golden sequences are byte-identical (`engineContract` bumped to 2 regardless). Eight dedicated tests in `personnel-state-value.test.mjs`; `BASELINE_V1.weights` updated in sync; full suite 1314/1314.

Not yet verified against the real loss: the tough+xenowarden-greenhorn sweep has not been re-run with this term active. And even if it closes that gap, it is the latest instance of the pattern this whole section exposes — a human doing two-move lookahead by reading a transcript, then hard-coding the answer. That is the argument for what comes next.

## 56. The combat-AI roadmap now lives in GitHub Issues

The full road from today's one-move-ahead evaluator to the strongest AI this project can realistically build — 2-ply lookahead, Monte Carlo Tree Search over the real engine's own sandbox, opponent models from the archetype library, then learned evaluation via self-play (Expert Iteration) — is recorded as an ordered, labeled set of 20 issues under milestone 1 of the project's repository, with **issue #3 as the epic checklist**: https://github.com/Garsondee/blank-inheritance/issues/3. Every issue cites the exact functions it reuses (`generateProposals`, `forkTurnSandbox`, `checkOutcome`, `evaluateState`, `decide()`, the worker pool) and declares its own tests and acceptance bar; #19 fixes the bar for "stronger" in advance (n ≥ 48 paired, ≥ 3 rosters, closes the §54 loss with `pileOnRisk` at 0). This document stays the record of *what was measured and decided*; the issues are the record of *what is planned*.

## 57. One AI for both scales — decided

Ingram, 2026-09-02: "Frame scale and 'personnel' scale should be the same system, we only need one AI not two... we're basing the frame scale on a slightly reflavoured and modified personnel scale but we're deliberately not changing the rules so much as to need two systems."

Recorded as a decision, not a proposal. The two trees are already near-twins by construction — `personnel-engine/decide.mjs`, `evaluate/state-value.mjs`, `knowledge/belief.mjs`, `world/pathfind.mjs`, `vision.mjs` are documented ports of the Frame-scale originals with one import swapped; the only geometric difference is `FEET_PER_SQUARE` (50 vs 5, §14f's "reskin, not a rebalance"). The real divergences are vocabulary and evaluator terms (Frame-only: Evacuate/Repair/Spot/Advance, `actionDenial`/`spotted`/`civilians`, damage-state action caps, the Claim reaction; personnel-only: the condition system, Athletics maneuvers, dying rules, whole-turn templates, `setupValue`/`mobilityDenial`/`maneuverTempoDenial`/`pileOnRisk`). So: **one decision layer, parameterized by an engine interface** (`feetPerSquare`, `generateProposals`, `forkTurnSandbox`, `checkOutcome`, `evaluateState`, initiative), with action vocabulary and evaluator features *registered by each engine*, and one ladder that runs at both scales. Convergence order and tests are in issue #20 (moved from "later" to a stage-0 constraint). This does **not** relax the §1–§3 scale-separation rule about kids and Frames sharing a battlefield — that is about bounded-accuracy maths, not about duplicating the brain.

## 58. Stage 0 lands: shared policy registry (issues #2, #4), and the Frame-scale closing bug (issue #24)

Three pieces of real, tested, merged stage-0 work, closing two GitHub issues outright.

**Weight-search confirmation battery (issue #2).** `runWeightSearch` gained optional `confirmSeedSpec`/`confirmPromotionOptions`: a candidate that clears `shouldPromote` at the cheap `seedSpec` is re-evaluated at a larger `confirmSeedSpec` before actually being promoted, with `confirmResults`/`confirmVerdict` recorded distinctly on history entries. Defaults to `null` (disabled) for library callers; the CLI defaults to `--confirmSeeds=1-24`. This is the direct fix for §54's own Chain B mirage (36 "promoted" candidates, all noise at n=24 re-verification) — 4 new tests, closed.

**The shared `EngineAdapter` and policy registry (issue #4; issue #20's own stage (a)).** `tools/battle-simulator/ai-common/` is a new, third layer — never merging `personnel-engine/` and `engine/`, per §1–§3's still-standing scale-separation rule — that defines the real contract both trees satisfy: `feetPerSquare`, `decisionRng`, `generateProposals`, `forkTurnSandbox`, `checkOutcome` (widened from module-private to exported in both `engine.mjs` files to make this possible, zero behavior change), `evaluateState`, `DEFAULT_WEIGHTS`, `nativeGreedyDecide`. A real policy registry (`registerPolicy`/`resolvePolicy`/`decideWithPolicy`) rejects an unknown or duplicate id loudly. `greedy-v1` is the first registered policy — pure dispatch to `adapter.nativeGreedyDecide`, zero new decision logic — wired into all three real call sites (`personnel-mirror.mjs`, `personnel-complex-battle.mjs`, `tournament-worker.mjs` transitively) via a new `"policy"` `decisionMode`, alongside the existing `random`/`worst`/`nearest-only` anchors. `personnel-engine/baselines.mjs` gained `GREEDY_V1_POLICY`/`POLICY_BASELINES` so `ALL_PROFILES["greedy-v1"]` resolves for any CLI caller — verified against the literal command `tournament-runner.mjs --party=baseline-v1 --opposition=greedy-v1`, both the sequential and worker-thread paths.

The "zero behavior change" claim is proven, not asserted: dedicated byte-identical-battle tests show `decideWithPolicy("greedy-v1", ...)` matches a direct `decide()` call action-for-action on both engines, including personnel-engine's own pinned golden sequence (`ai-profiles.test.mjs`, seed 42) reproduced through the full registry stack. Full battle-simulator suite: 1115/1115. Issue #4 closed. Issue #20 stays open — this is only its stage (a): the interface exists and both engines are reachable through it, but Frame's `ai/decide.mjs`/`ai/evaluate/state-value.mjs` still exist as separate, undeleted copies, and evaluator terms are not yet folded into shared registered features. Stages (b)–(d) remain real, open work.

**Frame-scale `closing` term dead-enemy exclusion (issue #24).** Found chasing #20's own Frame-scale research pass: `ai/evaluate/state-value.mjs`'s closing-gradient term (the §15k fix for a flat evaluator) still filtered its "nearest enemy" search to `enemy.currentHP > 0` — the exact monotonicity bug personnel-engine's own `closing` term already fixed for this identical mechanism (§32). Symptom: killing the currently-nearest, already-in-range enemy could make the search reassign "nearest" to a farther surviving one, retroactively penalizing the ally for "not closing" on a fight a teammate just finished — a direct §17 monotonicity violation. Fix: don't exclude dead enemies from the search (their position is frozen forever and `state.positions` never deletes on death at Frame-scale either, confirmed directly). Two new tests in `value-function-sanity.test.mjs`, including a real red/green check (temporarily reintroducing the old filter reproduces the failure, confirming the test actually catches the bug it's named for). 81/81 in the directly affected files, full suite unaffected.

## 59. Decision-cost profiling and a real budget API (issue #5)

"Nothing on this roadmap can be sized honestly without knowing what a decision costs today and having a real knob for how much a decision may cost tomorrow." Three real pieces, all shipped together, closing issue #5.

**The sandbox-fork counter.** A real, minimal, additive instrumentation hook added to `forkTurnSandbox` in BOTH `personnel-engine/engine.mjs` and `engine/engine.mjs`: `resetSandboxForkCount()`/`getSandboxForkCount()` around a module-private counter incremented on every real call. This is the one true seam a decision algorithm's real search cost passes through no matter how much caching sits above it — a real finding from researching this issue: `personnel-engine/decide.mjs`'s own `decide()` at `BASELINE_V1`'s default `competence: 1.0` does NOT do the naive one-fork-per-proposal pass `ai/decide.mjs` does; it delegates to `planTurn()`, which runs 5 turn-templates × up to 3 phases with a `scoredCache` that skips real `forkTurnSandbox` calls entirely on a cache hit. Counting at the fork site itself — rather than trying to derive a count from `generateProposals().length` at the call site — automatically and correctly reflects real work done regardless of which internal algorithm is live, with zero behavior change for every existing caller (a plain increment, never touching control flow or return value). Exposed as optional (non-required) fields on both `ai-common/` adapters.

**A real budget primitive (`createBudgetTracker`, `ai-common/engine-adapter.mjs`) and `budget-demo-v1` (`ai-common/policies/budget-demo.mjs`).** `options.budget` on the shared policy contract is `{ maxIterations, maxMs }`, either optional; `greedy-v1` never consults it (true by construction — JS drops an unused destructured field), so nothing about greedy needed to change. `budget-demo-v1` is a real, honest, MINIMAL anytime policy built purely to prove the contract end to end ahead of any real search policy (#1, #6) existing to exercise it: it examines legal candidates one at a time via the real `forkTurnSandbox`/`evaluateState` machinery, tracks the best-scored legal one, and stops once the budget is exhausted *and* it has a real answer in hand — never returning null just because an early candidate happened to be illegal (a real bug this policy's own dev cycle found and fixed, now a dedicated regression test). With an unlimited budget it converges to the exact same 1-ply-optimal choice `greedy-v1` makes immediately — it adds no strength of its own, only a budgeted route to the same answer; issues #1/#6 are where real strength from spending a larger budget comes from.

**The profiling harness (`analysis/decision-profile.mjs`).** Measures real per-decision wall time and real sandbox-evaluation count for a named policy on two fixed arenas — the small mirror arena, and the 8-combatant walled complex map (the exact real roster `personnel-decide.test.mjs`'s own PERFORMANCE test already uses) — instrumenting only the profiled side, appending to `battle-history.md` like every other analysis script. Real numbers gathered this session (seeds 1-4, roundLimit 6): mirror arena, greedy-v1, mean 2.93ms/91.8 sandbox evals per decision; complex map, greedy-v1, mean 281.71ms/131.7 sandbox evals per decision (max 1478ms on one real decision) — cost regressions are now as visible as win-rate regressions.

**The cost guard (`tournament-runner.mjs`'s new `--costGuard=` flag, `checkCostGuard`).** Opt-in (off by default, so no existing invocation's runtime changes): runs a real `greedy-v1`-vs-`greedy-v1` reference tournament with the identical roster/seeds/roundLimit, compares the tested tournament's real `meanElapsedMs` against a configurable multiple of the reference's, and warns in the same appended report a slow policy can't silently turn a short sweep into a very long one.

Every piece has real, direct tests — the counter's own zero-behavior-change proof, the budget tracker's exact iteration/wall-clock boundaries, `budget-demo-v1`'s convergence-to-greedy and anytime-cutoff proofs on both adapters, the profiler's real measurement + no-added-behavior + measures-something-real proofs, and the cost guard's exact boundary/exceeded/not-exceeded cases plus a real live tournament comparison. Full battle-simulator suite: 1121/1121.

## 60. A real 2-ply lookahead (issue #1) — a genuine trap caught, the diagnosed loss NOT closed

"Every piece of real tactical intelligence the bot has today is a rule a human noticed by reading a transcript and hand-coded as a new evaluator term... more hand-coded terms is whack-a-mole. The fix is depth, not more patches" (issue #1's own framing). Built and empirically tested; the honest result is a real negative, and that is itself the useful finding this issue asked for.

**What was built.** `lookahead-v1` (`ai-common/policies/lookahead.mjs`) — a real 2-ply search, registered as a NEW policy rather than a modification of `decide()` itself (a deliberate departure from the issue's literal "give decide() a lookahead" phrasing, explained in the policy's own header: `decide()` stays untouched so every golden sequence, every archetype profile, every prior measurement keeps its own real guarantee, and the identical practical outcome — a real, pluggable, testable algorithm usable anywhere `--opposition=lookahead-v1` already works — is achieved for free, on both engines, through the #4 registry). Algorithm: score every real candidate at 1-ply (byte-identical to `greedy-v1`), prune to the top `options.topK` (default 3), then for each simulate EVERY living enemy taking one cheap greedy reply in turn (`adapter.nativeGreedyDecide` at `competence: 0.99`, forcing personnel-engine's own real cheap one-shot path rather than the expensive `planTurn()` search — "the opponent model is simply 'they play like I do,'" issue #1's own phrase), and score by the state after those replies. `options.budget` (#5) bounds the 2-ply stage; never returns null while a real legal candidate exists. A documented simplification: every living enemy always gets exactly one reply, not gated by `state.initiativeOrder`'s real "who acts before my own next turn" — a real, separate refinement left for later, conservative in the sense that it can only ever look one reply further than the current moment strictly guarantees, never fewer.

**A real, verified trap.** Before writing any test, a real trap was found by direct empirical probing (not hand-derived): an unarmed combatant with a scripted objective at its own current square (so retreating costs real objective value) faces a melee enemy 5 squares off. `greedy-v1`'s 1-ply score walks TOWARD the enemy — the smaller objective-distance loss it buys outweighs the STATIC `threatExposure` estimate of the danger (1-ply score −2.34 vs retreating's −15, so approaching still "wins" despite the penalty). `lookahead-v1`'s real simulated reply (an actual Claw strike, not an estimate) reveals the true cost is nearly triple the static estimate (2-ply score −19.30 for approaching vs −15 for retreating) — enough to flip the decision. `greedy-v1` and `lookahead-v1` pick genuinely different, both-real-and-legal actions on the identical state. This is the direct, concrete proof depth changes a real decision, not just a number — `test/battle-simulator/lookahead.test.mjs`'s own THE TRAP tests.

**The test that actually matters — a real negative result.** Does `lookahead-v1` WITHOUT `pileOnRisk` (`LOOKAHEAD_V1_NO_PILEONRISK`, weights complete, `pileOnRisk` weighted to exactly 0) independently rediscover the pattern `pileOnRisk` was hard-coded for, and close the real, diagnosed 75% loss to `aggressive-v1` on `tough.json`+`xenowarden-greenhorn.json` (§54)? Measured properly — the §54 number (25.0% party win rate, n=24) is NOT the right control, since it used a different sample; the honest comparison is PAIRED, identical seeds, both run at n=48 this session:

- `baseline-v1` vs. `aggressive-v1`, seeds 1-48: **35.4%** win rate (17/48) — already noticeably higher than the old n=24 figure, itself a reminder that small samples move around.
- `lookahead-v1-no-pileonrisk` vs. `aggressive-v1`, IDENTICAL seeds 1-48: **33.3%** win rate (16/48) — statistically indistinguishable from baseline, arguably marginally worse, certainly not the decisive improvement a real "depth closes the gap" finding would show.

**Conclusion, stated as bluntly as the question was asked: no, this MVP 2-ply lookahead does not close the diagnosed loss.** Per the issue's own stated fork: "if it doesn't help, the evaluator itself — not the depth — is the bottleneck, and that's worth knowing before investing in issue #3 (MCTS)." This is a real, if modest, piece of that evidence — not conclusive on its own (one roster, one opponent, an MVP-depth search with real simplifications: only living-enemy-count many replies, a same-weights opponent model, no initiative-aware pruning), but it does NOT support "depth alone, cheaply added, fixes this specific evaluator-shaped hole." The genuine trap found above proves the MECHANISM works; the empirical test proves the specific diagnosed pattern (pile-on after a failed maneuver) is not the kind of thing THIS shallow, single-reply-per-enemy lookahead generically rediscovers — plausibly because the real pileOnRisk pattern needs the SECOND enemy's reply to be conditioned on the FIRST enemy's maneuver actually LANDING (a stochastic, correlated event this deterministic-expected-value 2-ply pass never represents as a distinct branch), not just "any living enemy gets one cheap reply."

**Real tests, full suite.** `lookahead-v1` registered, adapter-agnostic (personnel + Frame), never illegal, never null under a degenerate budget, `topK` genuinely prunes, plus the trap tests above. `LOOKAHEAD_V1_POLICY`/`LOOKAHEAD_V1_NO_PILEONRISK` registered in `ALL_PROFILES` the same way `GREEDY_V1_POLICY` was.

## 61. Stage 2 begins: the exactness oracle, Phase A (issue #21) — first component of MCTS, built alone and cross-validated

"Every serious MCTS implementation is validated against an exact search on positions small enough to solve. Without it, 'the search works' is a feeling; with it, it is a test" (issue #21's own framing). Ingram's explicit instruction going into stage 2: no need to rush, one component at a time — this section is that first component, deliberately scoped narrower than the full issue, with the narrowing explained rather than glossed over.

**What "Phase A" means, and why it's still a real ground truth, not a shortcut.** `personnel-engine/search/expectiminimax.mjs` is a depth-limited, exhaustive (no pruning, no sampling) minimax over the real node taxonomy issue #6 defines — decision nodes via the real `generateProposals`/`forkTurnSandbox`, terminal nodes via the real `checkOutcome`, the real two-team negamax reduction (side-relative `evaluateState`, max/min flips exactly when the acting side changes between parent and child, value always reported in the ROOT's own terms). What it does NOT yet do: real stochastic chance-node enumeration (expanding a strike/trip/etc. into its actual crit/hit/miss/crit-miss branches via `degreeProbabilities`) — that's issue #6's own step 4, a separate, real, future increment ("Phase B"). This narrowing is not arbitrary: `forkTurnSandbox` already resolves everything through `EXPECTED_OUTCOMES` (deterministic expected-value, never real dice), which is the EXACT SAME path `decide()`, `greedy-v1`, and `lookahead-v1` already use, and which design doc §52's own tactical puzzles are explicitly built and verified against ("every candidate scored inside decide() is expected-value only... which is precisely why a single seed is enough here"). A Phase-A oracle scoped to that same deterministic path is a genuine, correct ground truth for everything already built — not a lesser stand-in.

**Forced nodes — mostly real, one honest gap.** `runBattle`'s own end-of-turn machinery is replicated directly, not reimplemented from a guess: Stunned's exact action-loss arithmetic (`resolveEffectiveStats(combatant).actionsAvailable` minus `stunnedValue`, matching the real Player Core rule verbatim — a full stun skips the combatant's decision node entirely, exactly like `runBattle`'s own `continue` path), Frightened's end-of-turn decay, Grapple's round-based expiry, dead combatants skipped in initiative order. The one real gap: an UNCONSCIOUS combatant's own recovery check is a genuine d20 flat check (`resolveRecoveryCheck`) — a real stochastic forced node, not yet modeled. Encountering one mid-search stops that branch at its own current value rather than crashing or guessing; a dedicated test proves this degrades gracefully.

**Real validation, three independent angles, all agreeing:**
1. **Hand-computable**: a trivial 1-ply choice between a strong and weak strike on the same target — the oracle picks the real higher-gain one, and its own `value` field equals the real absolute `evaluateState` after that choice (a deliberate convention difference from `greedy-v1`/`lookahead-v1`'s own `score`, which is a GAIN/delta — the oracle reports the absolute position value, matching "the exact minimax value," not a relative improvement).
2. **Cross-validated against `decide()` itself**: run at depth 1 on design doc §52's own real "heal the critical ally" puzzle, the oracle independently reaches the identical real conclusion `decide()` does — two separately-built decision procedures agreeing on a real judgment call.
3. **Cross-validated against `lookahead-v1`'s own real trap (§60)**: given the identical single-reply comparison lookahead-v1 approximates (the acting combatant down to its last action this turn), the oracle at depth 1 reproduces `greedy-v1`'s own myopic pick, and at depth 2 — the enemy's real best reply now exhaustively explored rather than cheaply approximated — flips to the SAME retreat `lookahead-v1` already found. This is a genuinely independent confirmation neither piece of work had when it was first built.

A real methodological note, found and fixed before it became a silent bug in the test suite: with a fresh 3-actions-per-turn combatant, depth 2-3 can stay entirely within that ONE combatant's own turn and never reach an opponent's reply at all (`generateProposals`/`forkTurnSandbox` genuinely support the acting combatant taking several of its own actions in a row before anyone else moves) — the "2-3 ply" comparisons that actually matter (like the trap above) need the acting combatant already down to its last action, matching how `lookahead-v1`'s own single-action-at-a-time contract already works. Discovered by directly inspecting `nodesExplored`/`bestProposal` at increasing depth on a real 3-action turn and noticing the pick never changed — verified by hand before writing any test down, the same discipline this whole session has followed throughout.

**Registered as `exactness-oracle-v1`** (`ai-common/policies/exactness-oracle.mjs`) exactly as the issue asks — "so it can be tournamented at tiny depths as a sanity anchor" — personnel-scale only for now, throwing a clear, real error rather than silently misbehaving if ever resolved against `FRAME_ADAPTER`.

**What's deliberately NOT attempted yet** (real, open, not hidden): Phase B's stochastic chance nodes — meaning the oracle cannot yet compute a genuinely exact value for anything involving a real attack roll's own win/miss uncertainty, including issue #21's own headline example, "the exact 3-ply value of stepping into melee next to two Athletics-capable enemies" (the tough+xenowarden trip-pile-on trap) — that specific validation needs a real Trip-success probability weighted into the tree, which is precisely what Phase B adds. Frame-scale support (also real, separate work). 9 real tests, all passing; zero existing files touched (a purely additive first component); full battle-simulator suite unaffected.

## 62. Stage 2, second component: plain UCT MCTS, checked against the oracle

Step 2 of issue #6's own 8-step build order, still one component at a time: "decision + terminal nodes, deterministic transitions (EXPECTED_OUTCOMES), plain UCT, leaf = evaluateState. Test: converges to the oracle on the design doc §52 puzzles."

**Shared plumbing extracted first.** Before writing the tree search, the oracle's own node-taxonomy mechanics (`outcomeValue`, the deterministic forced-node handling, "who acts next") were pulled out of `expectiminimax.mjs` into `search/tree-shared.mjs` — not a rewrite, a real, zero-behavior-change extraction (the oracle's own 9 tests re-ran identically afterward), so the trickiest part of Phase A (Stunned/Frightened/Grapple/dead-combatant bookkeeping) is written and verified exactly once, not duplicated and re-risked in every new search algorithm this stage adds.

**`personnel-engine/search/mcts.mjs`** — a real, textbook-shape UCT tree: SELECT (UCT among expanded children, maximizing for the root's own team, mirrored-minimizing for the opponent — the same two-team negamax reduction the oracle uses, applied at selection time instead of a backprop sign flip), EXPAND (one real untried candidate via `forkTurnSandbox`, skipping any that turn out illegal), leaf value = `evaluateState` DIRECTLY on the newly expanded node — no rollout/simulation phase this step, exactly matching the build order's own words ("leaf = evaluateState"); a real playout policy is issue #8's own later, separate step. BACKPROPAGATE up the real path just walked, root-relative, no sign flip needed. Depth-capped (`maxDepth`), on purpose and unlike a "real" production MCTS, specifically so this search and the oracle explore the IDENTICAL horizon — the only way "converges to the oracle" is a fair, apples-to-apples test rather than two differently-shaped trees. The pick is the ROBUST child (most real visits — issue #6's own explicit anytime rule), not the raw highest mean.

**Real convergence, not an aspiration.** Run on both the hand-computable strike comparison and the §60/§61 trap scenario, MCTS's own picked action AND its own reported value match the Phase-A oracle's EXACTLY (not approximately — for these small, fully-explorable trees, MCTS converges all the way to the same exhaustive answer within a few hundred iterations). At `maxDepth=1` it reproduces the oracle's (and `greedy-v1`'s) myopic pick; at `maxDepth=2` it independently re-finds the same retreat `lookahead-v1` and the oracle both already found — now a THIRD, structurally different search method landing on the identical real conclusion. Also independently agrees with `decide()` itself on design doc §52's own "heal the critical ally" puzzle.

**Registered as `mcts-uct-v1`**, deliberately NOT `mcts-v1` — that id is reserved for the complete system issue #6's own build order produces once PUCT priors (#12), real chance nodes (#7), MCTS-Solver, and subtree reuse (#9) all land; naming this partial, no-rollout, depth-capped plain-UCT version "mcts-v1" now would overstate how far Stage 2 has gotten to anyone reading the registry later. A test asserts `mcts-v1` does NOT exist yet, so a future step that DOES register it will get a loud, obvious reminder to update this one.

7 real tests, personnel-scale only (matching the oracle's own current scope). Full battle-simulator suite unaffected.

## 63. Stage 2, step 3 closed out: turn order verified directly against `runBattle`

Issue #21's own explicit test for team/initiative correctness: "the oracle's `(round, actingId)` sequencing is checked against `runBattle` on a fixed seed once, then MCTS is checked against the oracle." The MECHANICS (Stunned/Frightened/Grapple/dead-combatant handling) were already real and tested via hand-built states in `expectiminimax.test.mjs`; this closes the one piece still missing — proof that `tree-shared.mjs`'s own `advanceToNextActor`/`nextActorAfterAction` reproduce a REAL, live battle's own turn order, not just a plausible-looking one.

**Design**: move-only combatants (no strikes/spells/abilities at all), so `forkTurnSandbox`'s `EXPECTED_OUTCOMES` path and a real battle's `REAL_OUTCOMES` path can never diverge — nothing stochastic ever gets resolved, since a pure move never rolls a die. One combatant carries a real partial `stunnedValue` (loses exactly 1 of 3 actions, Player Core's own rule) — the one forced-node case worth proving against a LIVE battle rather than only a synthetic state, since it's a real, common, and easy-to-get-subtly-wrong case. `runBattle` is run once on a fixed seed to get its own real `initiativeOrder` and turn log; a hand-walk built ENTIRELY from `tree-shared.mjs`'s own real functions plus the same real `decide()` policy `runBattle`'s own `ai` callback uses is then compared against it.

**A real mismatch found and correctly resolved, not silently worked around**: the first version of this test failed — not because the advancement logic was wrong, but because `runBattle`'s own log records ONE entry per WHOLE TURN (after all of a combatant's real actions), while this module's own decision nodes are per-ACTION ("nodes are action slots, not turns," design doc §61/#6's own words) — a combatant with 3 real actions produces 3 consecutive hand-walked entries for what `runBattle` itself logs as a single turn. The fix is a real, honest one: collapse consecutive repeats before comparing, which aligns two genuinely different real units (per-action decision nodes vs. per-turn log entries) rather than papering over an actual bug. Once compared at matching granularity, the sequences agree exactly, including the Stunned combatant staying correctly in sequence with its own reduced action count. A second, direct test confirms the bootstrap case specifically (`advanceToNextActor(state, order, -1, round)`, used to seed the very first actor in any search) applies a real Stunned reduction identically to a mid-search advance, not just to actors reached after the first.

2 real tests. Full battle-simulator suite unaffected. Step 3 of issue #6's own build order is now genuinely closed, not just "probably fine" — step 4 (real stochastic chance nodes, "Phase B" in §61/§62's own language) is the next real, substantive piece.

## 64. Stage 2, step 4: real stochastic chance nodes — and the headline validation finally computed

"The exact 3-ply value of stepping into melee next to two Athletics-capable enemies... with `pileOnRisk = 0`, is a number, not an opinion" (issue #21's own motivating example). This is the piece that makes that literally true.

**Scope, stated plainly.** Real chance-node enumeration for exactly two action types — `strike` and `trip` — the two the diagnosed trap actually needs. Every other stochastic action (shove, grapple, saveAttack, demoralize, conditionSave, areaAttack) still resolves through the Phase-A deterministic `EXPECTED_OUTCOMES` path; `isStochasticProposal` names exactly what's covered so nothing is silently mistreated. Extending coverage is real, additive, future work — the pattern is now established, not a one-off.

**One real engine change, small and load-bearing: `forkTurnSandbox` now accepts an optional `outcomes` table**, defaulting to `EXPECTED_OUTCOMES` exactly as before (a real, verified zero-behavior-change default — a dedicated test proves omitting the parameter and explicitly passing `undefined` produce byte-identical results). This is the ONE seam `REAL_OUTCOMES`/`EXPECTED_OUTCOMES` already established ("only the moment a die collapses to a number varies") — a new caller can now supply a THIRD such table without `attemptAction` (legality, action costs, multiple-attack-penalty/range/flanking/cover modifier computation, dying transitions, prone application) ever knowing or caring which table produced a given roll.

**`personnel-engine/search/chance.mjs`**: `makeDegreeForcedOutcomes(degree, report)` builds that third table — instead of a real roll or a continuous probability-weighted average, it resolves deterministically for ONE forced degree, with damage dice collapsed to their expectation for that degree (issue #6's own explicit, stated simplification — exact damage-die enumeration is real, separate, future work). `expandChanceNode` forks the sandbox once per real degree with `probability > 0` (up to 4), reading the REAL modifier/DC `attemptAction` itself computed back via `report` — never re-deriving multiple-attack-penalty/range/flanking/cover math a second time. Hand-verified exactly (not approximately) against manual computation for both a Strike (probabilities matched `degreeProbabilities(10,15)` to the decimal, damage matched `mitigateDamage` per degree) and a Trip (probabilities matched, and criticalSuccess/success/failure/criticalFailure each produced exactly the real prone/self-prone/damage Player Core's own rule specifies) before a single test was written down.

**Wired into the exactness oracle**: a stochastic proposal's own value is now the TRUE probability-weighted average of every branch's own recursively-searched value — the actual "expectiminimax" the module's name always promised, not "minimax over deterministic transitions" as Phase A shipped it. A real, non-obvious case caught immediately by the existing test suite: a lethal-on-crit strike now correctly folds the real WIN sentinel into its own weighted average (a crit branch that ends the fight is not just "high damage," it's a real terminal value) — an existing test's own old assertion (comparing the oracle's value to a single deterministic `evaluateState` call) failed the moment chance nodes landed, for the RIGHT reason, and was rewritten to hand-verify against `expandChanceNode` directly instead.

**The headline validation, finally computed — on the REAL diagnosed roster.** `tough.json` + `xenowarden-greenhorn.json` (design doc §54's own 75%-loss matchup), unmodified real fixtures, both real Athletics-capable and Strike-capable. A real ally (lunar-17's own real fort save/HP/AC, attack kit stripped to move-only with a scripted objective pulling it toward the pair — the same isolation technique the §60 lookahead trap already used, so the position stays "small enough to solve," issue #21's own words) faces the choice of approaching or retreating, with `pileOnRisk` weighted to exactly 0. Result: at depth 2 (seeing only the FIRST enemy's real reply), the search still prefers approaching. At depth 3 (the SECOND enemy's real reply now included), it correctly flips to retreating. This is a genuine, hand-verified, real-fixture re-derivation of the exact pattern `pileOnRisk` was hard-coded to price — computed by chance-node-aware search depth, not told the answer. A structurally similar single-enemy control also flips at depth 3, for a real but DIFFERENT reason (the search correctly seeing that danger compounds into the acting side's own next turn, not a second attacker) — reported honestly rather than folded into an overclaimed "proves pile-on" narrative; the two-enemy scenario's own depth-2-vs-depth-3 comparison is the clean, direct, pile-on-specific evidence.

12 tests in `expectiminimax.test.mjs` (2 new headline-validation tests, 2 rewritten for the new stochastic-value semantics), 6 in a new `chance.test.mjs`, 1 zero-behavior-change proof for the `forkTurnSandbox` widening. Full battle-simulator suite unaffected. MCTS's own chance-node wiring (a different design question — issue #6's own three-tier plan: enumerate near the root, sparse-sample deeper, open-loop in rollouts) is real, separate, deferred work, not attempted this pass.

## 65. Stage 2, step 5: PUCT + progressive widening — a measured, deterministic, "same-budget win"

Issue #6's own step 5: "PUCT priors + progressive widening + move-groups. Test: same-budget win over plain UCT on the puzzles." Built as its own module, `personnel-engine/search/mcts-puct.mjs`, alongside the existing plain-UCT `mcts.mjs` (kept completely unmodified — its own tests and `mcts-uct-v1` registration stay exactly as they were) — a genuinely new algorithm variant gets its own file and its own policy id, matching this whole stage's own established discipline.

**PUCT** (the AlphaZero selection rule): `Q(c) + c_puct * P(c) * sqrt(N) / (1 + n(c))` — an unvisited candidate is no longer automatically "infinitely interesting" the way plain UCT treats it; its real PRIOR `P(c)` decides how much attention it earns. The prior is exactly what the issue specifies: `softmax(greedyGain(a) / tau)`, where `greedyGain` is the SAME real one-ply `evaluateState(after) - evaluateState(before)` `decide()`'s own fallback path already computes — "the hand evaluator standing in for the policy network." `c_puct`/`tau` are real, defensible, DELIBERATELY UNTUNED defaults (1.5 and 10) — tuning them for real strength is issue #12's own explicit job, not this step's.

**Progressive widening**: only the top `ceil(k * N^alpha)` candidates BY PRIOR are eligible for selection at a node with `N` real visits so far (`k=2`, `alpha=0.5`, also untuned) — more candidates unlock as real visits accumulate, rather than every candidate being equally reachable from move one regardless of how unpromising its prior already looks.

**Deliberately NOT attempted this step, named plainly**: "move-groups" (collapsing near-duplicate movement proposals by class before scoring) — progressive widening already bounds branching-factor cost numerically without it, so this is real, additive, future work, not a prerequisite. Chance-node consumption during simulation is likewise still absent here, exactly as it is in `mcts-uct-v1` — a separate, still-unstarted piece of work for this whole search family, not specific to PUCT.

**The decisive test, found empirically before being written down.** On a real, unmodified-fixture position (lunar-17 facing `tough.json`+`xenowarden-greenhorn.json`, 20 real legal candidates — no stripping this time, the point was a genuinely large branching factor) at a tiny iteration budget SMALLER than the candidate count: plain UCT picks the real correct answer (matching the Phase-A oracle) **0 times out of 30 identical runs**; PUCT picks it **30 times out of 30**. Both search algorithms are FULLY deterministic in Phase A (no RNG anywhere — no rollout, no random tie-breaking), so those aren't 30 independent samples, they're 30 confirmations of one exactly-reproducible result, discovered by directly probing several budgets before trusting the number (the same "verify by hand before writing a test" discipline this whole project has followed throughout) — the 30-trial sweep in the actual test exists to be legible evidence, not because the outcome was ever in doubt once found. Swept across several small budgets (4/6/8/10 iterations), PUCT holds the correct answer at every one; both algorithms agree once the budget comfortably exceeds the real candidate count (200 iterations) — PUCT's real advantage is budget EFFICIENCY, not a different ceiling, exactly the honest claim this step makes.

7 tests: exact convergence to the oracle (same trap §60/§61 already used), the same-budget win above (both the single decisive case and the multi-budget sweep), real prior correctness (sums to 1, highest-gain candidate gets the highest prior), a direct proof progressive widening is genuinely gating candidates (not a no-op), terminal detection, and registration as `mcts-puct-v1` — deliberately NOT `mcts-v1` (a test asserts it still doesn't exist; chance-node consumption, MCTS-Solver, and subtree reuse are all still ahead). Full battle-simulator suite unaffected.

## 66. Stage 2, step 6: root trap insurance — the exact blunder issue #6 predicted, caught and corrected

Issue #6's own step 6: "MCTS-Solver + root alpha-beta trap check. Test: the §54 trip-pile-on position, with `pileOnRisk = 0`, chooses not to enter the trap." This section covers the trap-check half; MCTS-Solver (proven win/loss propagation, an efficiency concern near a resolved endgame rather than a correctness one) is real, separate, still-ahead work, not bundled in here just because the build order lists them together.

**Verified the real gap before building anything.** Before writing a line of insurance code, both existing MCTS variants were run directly on the real §64 trip-pile-on scenario (`tough.json`+`xenowarden-greenhorn.json`, `pileOnRisk=0`, depth 3) at 50/200/800 iterations. Both `mcts-uct-v1` and `mcts-puct-v1` confidently, consistently walk into the trap at every budget tested, including 800 iterations — never once converging to the oracle's own correct retreat. This is not a strawman or a contrived failure: it is exactly the class of blunder issue #6 itself predicted ("a highly selective MCTS is known to miss"), and the root cause is real and understood — neither MCTS variant has real chance-node awareness wired into its own tree search yet (only the Phase-A/B oracle does), so both treat Trip's own real ~40-50% success chance as if it always lands, understating how often the position is actually safe, while ALSO never getting to see the true probability-weighted cost of the ones where it doesn't.

**The mechanism** (`personnel-engine/search/trap-insurance.mjs`, Baier & Winands' own "MCTS-Minimax Hybrid" framing): MCTS already runs and produces a real answer via real visit counts. `applyTrapInsurance` takes MCTS's own top `topK` candidates (by real visit count — the same "robust child" signal MCTS itself already trusts) and asks the oracle's own `evaluateProposal` (a new, real export — refactored out of `expectiminimax.mjs`'s own per-candidate loop with zero behavior change, verified) what each is REALLY worth, `oracleDepth` plies deep, using the oracle's real chance-node machinery. Whichever of those top-K has the real highest oracle-verified value wins. Cost is bounded and real: a few hundred real nodes for a 2-candidate, depth-3 check, not a full re-search — a real, cheap insurance premium, not a redesign.

**The decisive result.** On the real trap, `mcts-puct-v1` alone (200 iterations) picks `move to (11, 10)` — walking closer. Trap insurance checks that pick plus the retreat candidate at oracle depth 3: the approach candidate's own real oracle value is **−175,035** (a large negative dominated by a real, substantial probability of the fight actually ending in a party defeat three plies deep — not just "somewhat worse," a genuine likely loss), against retreat's own real **−50.9**. Insurance correctly overrides, landing on the real retreat every time. A "first, do no harm" check on the original §60/§61 single-enemy trap (where PUCT already converges to the oracle unaided) confirms insurance does NOT spuriously override an already-correct pick. Registered as `mcts-puct-insured-v1` — the first policy in this whole search family that correctly avoids the real diagnosed trap.

6 tests (5 for the insurance mechanism itself, 1 for the registered policy), including the exact test issue #6's own build order names. Supports both real MCTS result shapes (`mcts.mjs`'s `root.children` Map and `mcts-puct.mjs`'s `root.candidates` array) identically. Full battle-simulator suite unaffected. MCTS-Solver remains real, separate, unstarted work for a later step.
